What currently stands

The most recent results whose criterion was met, each stated as its own ruling states it. A conclusion here is a conclusion about a mechanism, not about the campaign: none of these moved an acceptance condition.

  • Device time of the mm kernels under the document reduced backward. SUPPORTED against the frozen criterion, at 100 percent join coverage. aten::mm kernels whose launching cpu_op sits under the document-reduced path carry 221,559 us of the class 249,504 us over the 6 measured steps -- 88.80 percent, against a 25 percent line… evidence →
  • The per document weight gradient reduction is a material share of the mm launch count. SUPPORTED against the frozen 10 percent line, and by a wide margin. evidence →
  • The reduction site allocates a small fixed set of shared buffers not one per leaf. SUPPORTED against the frozen criterion, and the source says it plainly. ember_v0_document_reduction.py:93-94 at head 20338ddc are the body of reduce_weight_gradient, whose loop header at line 92 is "for start, end in document_spans(lengths, order)". evidence →
  • A python frame per allocation names the ember source line that made it. Of 34,986 frame-bearing allocation records, 16,945 are BACKWARD-TAGGED by the criterion's own definition (a frame named "backward" or a frame in torch/autograd/function.py). evidence →
  • Allocator frames for a backward allocation name the op not the autograd engine. Clause 1 refutes if the recorder admits no stack kind other than python: cuda/memory.py:1002 declares stacks: str = "all", so the non-python kind is not merely accepted, it is the DEFAULT. evidence →

Method

Every question runs the same cycle: an observation from the record, a mechanism that would explain it, a falsification criterion frozen before the measurement, the run, the ruling quoted against that criterion, and an explicit statement of what the result does not close. A criterion chosen after the numbers are in is chosen to fit them, so the tooling refuses a verdict on a hypothesis that has no frozen criterion and refuses a criterion that cannot fail.

Refutation is the useful outcome. Inconclusive is reserved for a run that did not discriminate — never for one whose answer was unwelcome. A refuted thread is not a dead end: each refutation carries the variants it does not speak to, so a later reader can tell what was actually closed.

Verdicts across the whole record: INCONCLUSIVE 10, REFUTED 24, SUPPORTED 40, open 5.

Figures and results

What a four-cycle FP16 thread actually established

A chain that produced three unwelcome verdicts and one finding that outranks all of them.

Research question: Does requesting FP16 accumulation on this card's dense contractions buy enough to matter, and if it does, what is the mechanism?  ·  carries the interactive figure, the joined sweep and kernel audit, and their limitations.

Full hypothesis ledger

Every hypothesis and prior attempt, searchable across its explanatory text rather than its identifier. Built to answer “has this already been tried?” before a new cycle opens.

Sessions

Work is recorded continuously; the session boundary is derived from the record itself rather than declared, so the boundary and the events cannot disagree. A campaign spans sessions and nothing resets at one.

Criterion, frozen before the run

Given the cycle-40 inputs (1,172.3 elementwise gaps retired, 46.7 optimizer gaps, 7,437.7 gaps per step, 32,700.0 us idle lever) that threshold is crossed at a measured ratio of about 1.406, so a ratio above ~1.406 refutes and a ratio at or below it supports. Evidence
state/issue1945-receipts/retired-gap-duration-ratio-20260915T0740Z.json
Result in full
The frozen criterion required the DURATION-WEIGHTED contested figure under RETIRES-EVERYTHING to be at or below 7,452.9 us per step. Measured 6,180.1 us, so SUPPORTED. MEASURED: mean gap 49.8641 us over all 7,437.7 gaps per step; mean gap 57.8055 us over the 3,446.0 elementwise-FOLLOWED gaps; RATIO 1.1593. Duration-weighted contention 5,974.9 us under RETIRES-NOTHING (stack 56,064.7, clears by 1,478.0) and 6,180.1 us under RETIRES-EVERYTHING (stack 55,859.5, clears by 1,272.9). The ratio would have to reach about 1.406 to refute; it is 1.159, so the verdict has about 21 percent of ratio headroom. THE DIRECTION IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT I PRE-STATED, AND THAT IS THE RESULT WORTH REPORTING. The docstring predicted a ratio BELOW 1.0 on the reasoning that clustered small kernels are what fusion targets and a kernel inside a dense launch cluster sits closer to its neighbour. The retiring gaps are LONGER on the mean, so cycle 40 count-share figure of 5,154.1 us UNDERSTATED the contention by about 820 us and cycle 40 worst-case margin drops from 2,093.7 us to 1,272.9 us. The composition still clears 1.5x under the hostile reading, which is what the criterion asked, but it clears by less than the predecessor said and the correction again runs against the direction that flatters me. THE MEAN AND THE MEDIAN DISAGREE IN SIGN, which is why both were published before the run. Median gap 19.4570 us over all gaps against 16.5752 us over the retiring ones, a median ratio of 0.852: the TYPICAL retiring gap is shorter, exactly as predicted, while the MEAN retiring gap is longer because a tail of long holes sits adjacent to elementwise kernels. Both are true and only the mean is the correct weight for this arithmetic, because a total equals a count times a MEAN -- so the verdict uses 1.1593 and the median is reported as the distributional finding rather than as an alternative number. THE REFUSAL WAS PROVED BEFORE THE GREEN RUN: --prove-red blanks the elementwise selector and the script exits 2 rather than publishing a zero or undefined ratio, reporting 44,626 gaps total so the trace and window scoping are known good.
Limitations, and what this does not close
A SHARPENING THAT RUNS AGAINST THIS VERDICT AND WAS NOT IN THE PRE-REGISTERED CAVEATS. The uniformity argument models the profiler as adding a fixed cost c per launch boundary to BOTH populations. Under that model the observed ratio is (m_e + c) / (m_a + c), which is pulled TOWARD 1.0 from whichever side it starts on. An observed ratio ABOVE 1.0 therefore cannot have been manufactured by additive uniform inflation out of a true ratio below 1.0, and the true ratio must be HIGHER than the measured 1.1593, not lower. So the additive model makes this measurement CONSERVATIVE in the direction that hurts: the real contention is at least 6,180.1 us and plausibly more, and the real margin is at most 1,272.9 us. The measurement cannot bound c from this trace, so no corrected number is published -- what is published is the sign of the bias, which is the part that matters for whether the verdict is safe. It is safe by 1,272.9 us with the bias pointing the wrong way and about 21 percent of ratio headroom before the flip. THE TWO PRE-REGISTERED WAYS THE UNIFORMITY ARGUMENT CAN FAIL STAND UNCHECKED, and neither is checkable from this trace: (1) if the profiler per-boundary cost SCALES with kernel duration rather than being fixed per launch it does not divide out at all, and the argument above about the sign of the bias goes with it; (2) if retiring kernels cluster near window edges or host synchronisation their gaps carry a different kind of time than a mid-chain gap and the ratio is not a clean correction. The mean/median sign split is direct evidence that the retiring population is NOT a uniformly rescaled copy of the whole, which makes (2) more live than it was before this run, not less: something is putting long holes next to elementwise kernels and nothing here says what. WHAT THIS DOES NOT DISCHARGE. The governed-basis gap-duration measurement remains OWED. This cycle refines the number by measuring a ratio within one profiled trace; it does not settle the durations, and the separation clause still forbids composing absolute profiled durations with a governed figure. The optimizer share is NOT ratio-corrected because nothing measures that population own duration ratio; leaving it at the count share is conservative only if the optimizer population is not itself long-tailed, which is unmeasured. ALSO STILL OPEN AND NOW THE BINDING WEAKNESS: all three levers remain MAXIMAL-EFFECTIVENESS ceilings rather than measured effects, so any arm this stack licenses is licensed on ceilings. No matched-run variation exists for any of it, so the governed-run evidence contract repeatability clause stays unsatisfied. Cycles 38 through 41 have now spent four cycles refining one correction term whose total movement is about 1,026 us of a 62,039.6 us stack; the contention question is closed and the ceiling-versus-measured-effect question is where the next cycle belongs.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF the DEPENDENCY hole COUNT share, on whichever of the two populations is most favourable to the composition, EXCEEDS 0.0480 -- equivalently if the three-lever stack falls below 54,586.7 us per step once the idle lever is scaled to the holes it can actually reach. THE THRESHOLD IS DERIVED FROM THE BAR AND FROM NOTHING READ IN ANY RECEIPT: 1.5x on the 0.16376 s governed step needs 54,586.7 us removed; the elementwise and optimizer levers supply 29,339.6 us between them; cycle 41 measured the contested overlap at 0.18899 of whatever the idle lever is, so the idle lever must supply 25,247.1 / 0.81101 = 31,130.2 us of reachable idle out of its priced 32,700.0, which is 95.2% of it. Any dependency share above 4.80% therefore refutes. ALSO REFUTED, independently of any share, IF THE CORRELATION JOIN FAILS: the instrument exits non-zero when every hole is UNATTRIBUTED, or when more than 10% of holes are, rather than publishing a dependency share of zero -- because a zero would read as "no idle is dependency-bound", hand the idle lever its full 32,700.0 us, and be indistinguishable in arithmetic from a broken join. I EXPECT THIS TO REFUTE, and I am saying so before the run rather than after, because I have already read the split receipt's hole counts. That is exactly why the threshold above is computed from the bar rather than chosen, and why the verdict binds on the population most favourable to the thing I expect to refute.

Evidence
state/issue1945-receipts/idle-lever-dependency-floor-20260915T0825Z.json
Result in full
The frozen criterion said REFUTED IF the DEPENDENCY hole COUNT share, on whichever population is most favourable to the composition, EXCEEDS 0.0480. Measured 0.3642 on the favourable population and 0.3677 on the other -- SEVEN AND A HALF TIMES the threshold, so REFUTED, and not marginally. MEASURED, over 6 windows: on the all-device population, 7,906.7 interior holes per step of which DEPENDENCY_HOST_BUSY 2,766.7 and DEPENDENCY_HOST_IDLE 113.3 against HOST_DISPATCH 5,026.7; on the kernel-only population that cycles 38-41 priced, 7,437.7 holes of which 2,625.7 + 109.5 dependency against 4,702.5 host-dispatch. Both populations agree to within 0.0035 of each other, so the answer does not turn on which one the 32,700.0 us figure covers. THE ARITHMETIC IT FORCES: the idle lever can reach 20,789.0 us of its priced 32,700.0; re-applying cycle 41 measured contested share of 0.18899 to the smaller lever gives 3,928.9 us contested; the three-lever stack becomes 46,199.7 us against a 54,586.7 us requirement, SHORT BY 8,386.9 us. On the 0.16376 s governed step that stack is about 1.393x, below the 1.5x floor that 1945-multiplicative-movement-only clause 1 requires before any arm may be dispatched. THE COUNT AND THE DURATION DISAGREE BY MORE THAN AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE and the disagreement is the evidence, not a problem: dependency is 36.4% of holes by COUNT and 2.3% by profiled DURATION, because the profiler inflates exactly the host-side time that HOST_DISPATCH holes are made of. Count survives profiling, duration does not -- the principle cycles 38-41 established, pointing the other way here. Anyone reading the duration share would conclude the idle lever is almost entirely reachable, and that reading is an artifact of the instrument. THE BIAS RUNS AGAINST THE REFUTATION, which is the direction that makes it safe to act on: profiling lengthens every launch call, so a launch that truly returned before a hole can appear still-open and be misclassified as HOST_DISPATCH, never the reverse. 0.3642 is a LOWER BOUND on the true dependency share and 46,199.7 us is an UPPER BOUND on the stack. I PREDICTED THIS REFUTATION IN WRITING BEFORE THE RUN, and disclosed in the same breath that I had already read the prior split receipt hole counts -- which is why the threshold was computed from the bar (54,586.7 needed, 29,339.6 from the other two levers, cycle 41 contested share) rather than chosen, and why the verdict binds on the population most favourable to the thing I expected to refute. The deliberate red was proved first: dropping every launch correlation makes all 44,626 holes UNATTRIBUTED and the script exits 2 rather than publishing a dependency share of zero, which would have handed the idle lever its full 32,700.0 us.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE, and the not-closed half is larger than the refutation. A DEPENDENCY hole is NOT unreachable idle. It is idle unreachable by THIS lever as priced -- the launch-rate lever, which makes launches cheaper or fewer. The device sitting idle with its next kernel already issued is the definition of work serialized behind other work, and that is precisely the population the whole-step graph with the optimizer on a second stream targets. That is row 3 of the factor-dominant table (~32 ms, 19% of the 4x), it is one of the two rows nobody has touched, and it is a SEPARATE lever that this cycle has just SIZED rather than retired: 2,880.0 holes per step, 36.4% of all interior holes, with their durations unmeasured on any governed basis. So the honest reading is not that the idle is gone, it is that the idle was assigned to the wrong lever. WHAT IT DOES CLOSE. The three-lever composition -- idle + elementwise + optimizer -- does not reach 1.5x. It reaches about 1.393x on the governed step under the most favourable population, with the measurement bias pointing further down. Under 1945-multiplicative-movement-only clause 1 no arm may be dispatched whose pre-stated ceiling is below 1.5x, so the arm cycles 38-41 were converging on is NOT LAWFUL TO DISPATCH as composed. Four cycles of contention refinement were spent on a term worth about 1,026 us while the lever itself was overstated by 11,911.0 us, and the correction that mattered was one measurement away the whole time. That is the cost of refining the term that was already measured instead of the ceiling that never was. THIS SELECTS A ROUTE AND RULES NOTHING ABOUT THE TARGET. The bar is unchanged, no gate relaxes, and no impossibility is claimed or implied: measurement selects the route, never the exit, and only the operator rules impossibility from receipts. WHAT IS NOW UNVERIFIED AND WAS NOT BEFORE. The other two levers have had no such audit. The elementwise lever's 19,105.6 us assumes every intermediate stays in registers; the optimizer's 10,234.0 us is distance to a bandwidth floor computed under a PUBLISHED rate, not a measured one. Each is a ceiling of exactly the kind this cycle just found overstated by 36%, and neither has been scaled by anything measured. The idle lever's 32,700.0 us base figure itself is inherited and was not re-derived here -- this cycle scaled it. STILL OPEN THROUGHOUT: one profiled trace, one geometry, no matched-run variation, so nothing here supports an improvement or ceiling claim under the governed-run evidence contract.

the-idle-lever-prices-idle-it-cannot-reach-dependency-bound-holes-20260915 · 2026-09-15T15:21:02Z → 2026-09-15T15:23:48Z · full entry
The elementwise lever credits intermediates that cannot stay in registersdiagnostic, existing captureThe byte half of the lever credits each kernel with shedding ONE traversal of its largest tensor -- the single crossing of the intermediate it produces or consumes.The frozen criterion was: REFUTED IF the unreachable charge on the ISOLATED set EXCEEDS 7,452.9 us per step -- the margin the composition ever had, computed from the bar in cycle 38 and reused unchanged through 39, 40 and 41.SUPPORTEDWHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE, and the largest item is bigger than what the cycle settled.
Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF the unreachable charge on the ISOLATED set EXCEEDS 7,452.9 us per step. THE THRESHOLD IS FROZEN FROM THE BAR AND IS NOT CHOSEN HERE: 1.5x on the 0.16376 s governed step needs 54,586.7 us removed, the naive three-lever sum supplied 62,039.6 us, so the margin the composition ever had is exactly 7,452.9 us -- the same figure cycles 38, 39, 40 and 41 used unchanged. A lever whose unreachable part alone exceeds the entire margin the composition ever held is not a ceiling that was slightly optimistic; it is a ceiling of the same kind cycle 42 found overstated by 36%. ALSO REFUSED, independently of any share, IF THE CLASSIFICATION DEGENERATES: the instrument exits non-zero when the ISOLATED set is EMPTY, because an unreachable charge of 0.0 would read as "every credited microsecond is fusible" -- a FINDING that leaves the lever at its full 19,105.6 us -- while being equally consistent with a neighbour lookup that never resolves, which is a DEFECT; and when EVERY elementwise kernel is isolated, which with this class holding roughly half of all kernels is a broken timeline join rather than a fact about fusion. A sized coverage below 0.50 also refuses, because a thin sized population makes every isolated kernel charge nothing and the lever would pass for the one reason that is not evidence. I DO NOT PREDICT THE DIRECTION OF THIS ONE, and I am saying so before the run because I predicted cycle 42's refutation in writing and that is only creditable if I also say when I cannot. The elementwise class holds roughly half the kernels in the step, which argues that most of them have an in-class neighbour and the lever largely survives; against that, the class's largest credited sites are aten::copy_ and aten::add_ on residual-stream shapes, which sit between GEMMs by construction. Those two considerations point opposite ways and I have not measured which dominates.

Evidence
state/issue1945-receipts/elementwise-fusibility-floor-20260915T0835Z.json
Result in full
The frozen criterion was: REFUTED IF the unreachable charge on the ISOLATED set EXCEEDS 7,452.9 us per step -- the margin the composition ever had, computed from the bar in cycle 38 and reused unchanged through 39, 40 and 41. Measured 4,141.1 us per step, which is 55.6% of that threshold, so the criterion is met and the verdict is SUPPORTED. THE PARTITION. Of 3,447.0 elementwise kernels per step holding 27,447.4 us of device time, 2,700.0 kernels (20,840.1 us device, 14,479.6 us credited) have at least one in-class device neighbour and so have a candidate fusible edge; 747.0 kernels (6,607.3 us device, 4,625.9 us credited) have neither device neighbour in the class, so no fusion within this class can hold their intermediate in a register. Charging those isolated kernels only their eliminable-byte credit and letting them keep min(dur, excess) gives the 4,141.1 us. Isolated kernels are 21.67% of the class by count and carry 24.21% of its credited time. THE LEVER WAS RECOMPUTED, NOT ASSUMED: the capped composite from the inherited byte model comes out at 19,105.6 us per step against the 19,105.6 us the composition prices, with the two halves at 7,017.0 excess and 13,703.8 eliminable-byte pre-cap and sized coverage 0.9960. The audit therefore describes the same lever the composition uses, to the last microsecond. SUPPORTED IS ABOUT THE LEVER AND NOT ABOUT THE COMPOSITION, and the composition moved the other way. The elementwise lever falls from 19,105.6 to 14,964.5 us and the three-lever stack falls from cycle 42's 46,199.7 to 42,058.6 us against the 54,586.7 required -- short by 12,528.1 us, about 1.346x on the 0.16376 s governed step. Cycle 42 already ruled that composition REFUTED; this cycle lowers it further and rules only that the remaining shortfall is not attributable to the adjacency half of this lever's premise. THE BIAS RUNS THE SAME WAY IT DID IN CYCLE 42. Adjacency over-counts fusibility, because two adjacent elementwise kernels need not be dataflow-connected. So GROUPED is an upper bound on the genuinely fusible set, 4,141.1 us is a LOWER bound on the unreachable credit, and 14,964.5 us is an UPPER bound on the audited lever. A SUPPORTED verdict computed from a lower bound on the damage is the weaker direction of this instrument, and that is stated rather than glossed.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE, and the largest item is bigger than what the cycle settled. (1) THE EXCESS HALF IS A ONE-SIDED RESIDUAL AND IS UNAUDITED. 7,017.0 us of the lever is sum over kernels of max(0, dur - bytes_time) at a PUBLISHED 1008.0 GB/s reference. The clamp is per kernel, so a kernel faster than the reference contributes 0 rather than a negative, while a slower one contributes its full shortfall. The class as a whole achieves 1073.6 GB/s -- ABOVE the reference -- so the two-sided aggregate residual is negative and 7,017.0 us is the sum of one tail of a distribution straddling zero. Whether that tail is removable overhead or is kernels whose working set exceeds the 72 MB L2 is unmeasured, and it is the named successor. (2) REGISTER AND SHARED-MEMORY CAPACITY IS STILL ENTIRELY UNMEASURED, and adjacency does not touch it. A GROUPED kernel whose intermediate is a [4096, 32768] tensor is 256 MiB at bf16; nothing holds that in registers, so having an in-class neighbour is necessary and nowhere near sufficient. The 14,479.6 us credited to grouped kernels is bounded by this cycle from below only. (3) NO DATAFLOW EDGE IS PROVEN. Adjacency on the device timeline is a proxy; the trace carries no producer-consumer relation between tensors. (4) THE OPTIMIZER LEVER, 10,234.0 us, has had no ceiling audit at all. It is distance to a bandwidth floor computed under the same published rate this cycle just found the elementwise excess half resting on, so the two share a premise. (5) The three levers are still priced against ONE capture, one process, under profiling instrumentation, with no matched-run variation -- so under the governed-run evidence contract none of them is quotable as a measured ceiling for a governed run. WHAT THE CYCLE ALSO CORRECTED, recorded because it is a correction of my own published prose rather than of an instrument. The cycle-42 ledger block asked whether the 19,105.6 us figure "already contains launch/idle time the idle lever ALSO counts". Reading the source settles it: the per-kernel credit is min(dur_us, excess + eliminable_byte_time) summed over kernel DEVICE durations, so no host time and no inter-kernel hole can enter it. The same block quoted "16.3 us per removed kernel ... far above a typical elementwise kernel's device time" as an anomaly; that divided the whole lever by only the fully-removed count. The receipt's own split is 1,172.3 kernels removed outright for 8,634.1 us, or 7.365 us each, which is entirely ordinary. There was no anomaly and the sentence should not have been published.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF the one-sided excess sitting on DRAM_BOUND kernels -- those whose sized working set exceeds the 72 MB L2 capacity -- EXCEEDS 4,730.5 us per step. THE THRESHOLD IS A ROUTE-SELECTION THRESHOLD AND IS STATED AS SUCH RATHER THAN DRESSED AS THE BAR. The 1.5x question is already settled: cycle 42 ruled the three-lever composition REFUTED at 46,199.7 us against 54,586.7 needed and cycle 43 lowered it to 42,058.6 us. No further reduction of any lever can change that verdict, so a threshold derived from 1.5x could only be a criterion that cannot fail, which the loop forbids. The live question is which lever the campaign should pursue next, and the threshold that changes that answer is the one that REORDERS the two audited levers: the elementwise lever stands at 14,964.5 us after cycle 43 and the optimizer lever at 10,234.0 us, so a double-counted charge above their difference of 4,730.5 us drops elementwise below optimizer and inverts the campaign's own ranking. ALSO REFUSED, independently of any charge, IF THE PARTITION DEGENERATES: the instrument exits non-zero when the DRAM_BOUND set is EMPTY, because an excess that is 100% admissible overhead would read as "the estimator is sound" -- a FINDING that leaves the excess half fully spendable -- while being equally consistent with a size join that resolved nothing, a DEFECT; and when EVERY kernel is DRAM_BOUND, which contradicts the class achieving above the published rate and is a broken join rather than a finding. Sized coverage below 0.50 also refuses, and that is the branch proved red before this cycle opened. I DO NOT PREDICT THE DIRECTION. The class's largest sites include aten::copy_ and aten::fill_ at [4096, 32768], which is 256 MiB at bf16 and far above L2, and those carry real device time; against that, most of the 3,447.0 kernels per step are small residual-stream and expert shapes well inside 72 MB. Which population holds the positive residual is what the run decides.
Instrument
scripts/issue1945/elementwise_excess_two_sided.py over cia-captured-trace-20260914T183257Z.json, with the byte model, the elementwise selector and the published reference IMPORTED from elementwise_byte_reduction_ceiling so the one-sided reading reproduces the 7,017.0 us the composition spends. Deliberate red PROVED BEFORE this cycle opened: --prove-red drops every kernel-to-cpu_op correlation, sized coverage falls to 0.0000, and the instrument exits 2 rather than publish an excess that is 100% admissible overhead. Limitations, and what this does not close
THE FINDING THIS CYCLE PRODUCED OUTSIDE ITS OWN CRITERION, AND IT IS LARGER THAN THE ONE IT RULED. The three readings of the estimator are: one-sided at the published rate +7,017.0 us per step; TWO-sided at the same rate MINUS 1,668.5 us per step; one-sided at the class's own measured 1073.6 GB/s +7,548.0 us, two-sided there +110.1 us. Per step 2,253.3 kernels carry a positive residual and 1,193.7 a negative one. So the per-kernel residual is a distribution centred within about 110 us of zero once the reference is the class's own rate, and the 7,017.0 us the composition spends is the positive half of it. That is DISPERSION, not a systematic shortfall, and the per-kernel clamp is what converts one into the other: it manufactures an 8,685.5 us difference between the one-sided and two-sided readings of the same population. WHY THAT IS NOT RULED HERE. The frozen criterion asked whether DRAM traffic explains the excess. It does not, by 151.2 us. Ruling the dispersion finding under this cycle would be choosing a criterion after seeing the numbers, so it is recorded and named as the successor instead: whether the positive dispersion is collectible at all, or whether a kernel slower than its class mean is simply a different shape or dtype with a genuinely lower achievable rate, which no fusion collects. WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE. (1) The dispersion question above, which is 7,017.0 us of a 14,813.3 us lever -- 47% of it. (2) THE L2 TEST IS A WORKING-SET TEST, NOT A RESIDENCY MEASUREMENT: no L2 hit-rate counter exists in this build, a kernel under 72 MB can miss L2 because it shares the cache with everything in flight, and a kernel above it can hit on part of its traffic. The two errors run opposite ways and I cannot sign the net, which is why this cycle carries no one-directional bias argument where cycles 42 and 43 both did. (3) Register and shared-memory capacity remains entirely unmeasured, so cycle 43's 14,479.6 us credited to grouped kernels is still bounded from below only. (4) THE OPTIMIZER LEVER, 10,234.0 us, still has had no audit, and it is distance to a bandwidth floor computed under this same published rate -- which this cycle has now shown the class as a whole EXCEEDS by 6.5%, so the optimizer figure inherits the question directly. (5) One capture, one process, under profiling instrumentation, no matched-run variation.

The frozen criterion was: REFUTED IF the within-site one-sided residual over multi-invocation sites EXCEEDS 4,579.3 us/step. Measured 1,319.1 us/step, 28.8% of the threshold and 18.8% of the priced 7,017.0 us. SUPPORTED. The class partitions into 118 sites and 100.0% of class device time sits in multi-invocation sites, so the degeneracy refusal did not fire and the partition is computed over the whole population rather than a corner of it; single-invocation sites hold 0.0 us/step. Class-wide reading reproduces the priced figure exactly (one-sided 7,017.0, two-sided -1,668.5, coverage 0.9960), so the instrument is measuring the same quantity the composition spends. Judged against its own site mean, the two-sided residual is -0.0 us/step by construction and the BETWEEN-SITE remainder is 5,697.9 us/step -- 81.2% of the priced excess is the statement that some shapes run slower than the class average, which is a property of the shape and is collectible only by changing shapes. I predicted SUPPORTED in writing before the run and the prediction is the direction that shrinks my own lever.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS RETIRES, AND IT IS THE LARGEST SINGLE RETIREMENT OF THE AUDIT. A fusion collects between-site heterogeneity only by changing the shapes, and changing the shapes is exactly what the BYTE half of this same lever prices -- 13,703.8 us of eliminable intermediate traversals. Crediting the between-site excess as well charges the same mechanism twice, which is the double count the per-kernel cap in the original instrument was written to prevent and did not catch, because the cap bounds a kernel against its own duration and cannot see that its two halves name one act. So 5,697.9 us/step comes off: elementwise lever 14,813.3 -> 9,115.4 us/step; three-lever stack 41,907.4 -> 36,209.5 of the 54,586.7 required, short by 18,377.2, about 1.28x. THE RANKING FLIPS, WHICH IS WHAT THE THRESHOLD WAS FOR. The elementwise lever now stands BELOW the optimizer lever (9,115.4 against 10,234.0) for the first time in this campaign. Three cycles ago it was 19,105.6 and was the largest of the three. A DEFECT IN MY OWN FROZEN CRITERION, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY PASSED. I froze the threshold against the SURVIVING within-site residual and labelled it "the threshold that reorders the two levers". The quantity that actually reorders them is the RETIRED amount, so the exact flip condition is within-site < 2,437.7 us/step, not <= 4,579.3. The two are not the same number and I wrote them as though they were. The measured 1,319.1 clears BOTH readings, so the verdict and the reordering both stand on the arithmetic rather than on the looser wording -- but a criterion whose stated meaning and stated number disagree is a defect even when the result is insensitive to it, and it would not have been insensitive at 3,000 us. WHAT SURVIVES AND IS NOT CLOSED. The 1,319.1 us/step of within-site dispersion is NOT established as removable overhead and this cycle does not claim it is. Scheduling, clock and power state, L2 occupancy from neighbouring work, and measurement noise all produce within-site dispersion, and no counter in this build separates them from per-kernel launch cost. The number bounds where overhead COULD live -- 1,319.1 us/step, 4.8% of the class device time -- and nothing more. Also untouched: sites are keyed on recorded input dims, so two genuinely different workloads sharing a dims string merge into one site and inflate the within-site figure, which means the true within-site dispersion is at or below 1,319.1 and the retirement is if anything understated. THE SUCCESSOR IS THE OPTIMIZER LEVER AND IT INHERITS THIS EXACT QUESTION. 10,234.0 us is the last of the three with no audit at all, and it is distance to a bandwidth floor computed under the same published 1008.0 GB/s reference that this class exceeds by 6.5%. A floor computed under a rate the hardware beats is not a floor, and after this cycle it is also the LARGEST remaining lever.

Result in full
The frozen criterion was: REFUTED IF the recomputed optimizer lever EXCEEDS 9,115.4 us/step, the elementwise lever after cycle 45. Measured 11,468.8 us/step. REFUTED. The calibration set cleared both refusal floors -- 18 pure-movement kernels above L2 holding 14,451.5 us of device time against floors of 10 kernels and 1,000 us -- so the empty-set branch, proved red before the cycle opened, did not fire. Substituting a rate measured on this card for the specification figure does not shrink the optimizer lever; it makes it LARGER, because the measured streaming rate is above 1008.0 GB/s rather than below it, so the floor falls from 6,583.8 to 5,348.9 us. The hostile median-rate variant lands at 11,279.7 us, on the same side of the threshold, so the frozen INCONCLUSIVE clause -- max and median straddling 9,115.4 -- did not fire either. The optimizer remains the campaign's largest lever and the only one whose distance has never been attacked on its own terms.
Limitations, and what this does not close
I PREDICTED REFUTED AND I WAS RIGHT FOR THE WRONG REASON, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE VERDICT. My registered basis was that the empirical rate would land BELOW the published 1008.0, because the pure-movement class as a whole runs at 778.4 GB/s. It lands ABOVE: 1240.7 max, 1198.3 median. The class figure is dragged down by SMALL movement kernels where launch cost dominates the duration, and the calibration set deliberately excluded exactly those. So the direction of my prediction survived and its mechanism did not, and a prediction whose reasoning fails is a prediction that got lucky. THE FINDING IS LARGER THAN THE VERDICT AND IT POINTS AT THE BYTE MODEL. A measured achieved rate of 1240.7 GB/s is 23% ABOVE this card's published peak, on kernels selected precisely because their working set exceeds L2 and their byte accounting is the least arguable in the class. A rate above the physical peak is not a fast kernel; it is an arithmetic that has attributed more bytes to a kernel than the kernel moved, or traffic that was served from cache despite a working set larger than the cache. Either reading is a defect in the DERIVED byte model, and that model is not a detail of this cycle -- it is what produced the 13,703.8 us eliminable-bytes half of the elementwise lever, the single largest quantity in the entire audit. SO THIS CYCLE DOES NOT BANK ITS OWN NUMBER. 11,468.8 us is a LARGER lever than the priced 10,234.0 and therefore the more favourable figure for the campaign, and it rests on a reference rate this same cycle has just found reason to distrust. The optimizer lever stays at 10,234.0 pending the byte-model audit, and the three-lever stack stays at 36,209.5 of the 54,586.7 required. Adopting a favourable number computed on a basis I have just impeached would be the exact move the campaign has been correcting for three days. WHAT IS NOT CLOSED, AND ONE PIECE OF IT SPANS THE THRESHOLD. The reference set's rates run from 798.6 to 1240.7 GB/s and the rate at which the two levers swap places is 861.6 GB/s, so a reference drawn from the low end of the observed spread -- or from the whole pure-movement class at 778.4 -- would have produced SUPPORTED. The frozen criterion named max and median, both landed on the same side, and the verdict follows the criterion as frozen; the sensitivity is recorded here UNRULED rather than used to re-cut the verdict after the numbers were in. Also untouched: no MEASURED device-memory traffic exists for any kernel in this campaign, so both the optimizer footprint and the calibration bytes are derived quantities that the governed-run evidence contract requires to be reported separately from measured traffic; and the optimizer is a multi-tensor kernel walking 702 moment tensors rather than a contiguous copy, so if its access pattern is less efficient than the calibration set's its true floor is HIGHER and its true lever SMALLER than any reading here. THE SUCCESSOR IS THE BYTE MODEL ITSELF, and it is the first cycle of this audit that attacks a quantity rather than a lever: whether the derived byte accounting over-counts, established by the fact that it yields achieved rates above the card's physical peak, and by how much.

Result in full
The frozen criterion was: SUPPORTED IF more than 10% of above-peak device time sits on kernels whose achieved rate exceeds their own cache-adjusted ceiling, peak * bytes / (bytes - 72 MiB). Measured 0.6544 -- 9,665.7 us of 14,770.7 us. SUPPORTED, at more than six times the threshold. The above-peak population is 809 kernels holding 14,770.7 us, far above the floor of 10, so the thin-population refusal proved red before the cycle opened did not fire. The eleven kernels cache cannot explain are all aten::copy_ at exactly 1,073,741,824 bytes, running 1194.8 to 1240.7 GB/s against a ceiling of 1084.2: a 1 GiB traversal can be at most 7% resident in a 72 MiB cache, so no residency assumption reaches those rates. This is the STRONG direction under a bound built to be maximally generous to the cache explanation, and it is the OPPOSITE of the REFUTED I registered in writing before the run.
Limitations, and what this does not close
THE BOUND FALSIFIES A CONJUNCTION AND THE HYPOTHESIS NAMED ONLY ONE OF ITS TERMS. The ceiling assumes three things at once: the byte count is right, the kernel duration is right, and 1008.0 GB/s is this card's peak. Exceeding it falsifies their conjunction, not any single term. My hypothesis named the byte model, the criterion was frozen on it, and the verdict follows the criterion -- but the artifact does not get to convert that into a proof that bytes are the guilty term, and this update says so before anything downstream quotes it. THE MAGNITUDE POINTS AWAY FROM THE BYTES, AND THAT IS THE MOST USEFUL THING HERE. To bring 1240.7 under its 1084.2 ceiling the byte count would have to be inflated by a factor of 1.144. A byte model that miscounts traversals is wrong by a WHOLE TRAVERSAL -- 2x, 1.5x, 1.33x for two, three or four traversals counted as one more or one fewer. There is no traversal arithmetic that produces 14.4%. So the shape of the error does not look like the mechanism my own hypothesis named, and the term that would explain it cleanly is the REFERENCE: if this card sustains more than 1240.7 GB/s on a 1 GiB device-to-device copy, every one of these kernels is innocent and so is the byte model, and what is wrong is the specification figure the whole campaign divides by. WHY THAT IS NOT A LOOPHOLE I GET TO TAKE TODAY. 1008.0 GB/s is what a 384-bit bus at 21 Gbps yields, and a sustained rate 23% above it is not something a memory controller does. The remaining candidates are memory compression on the copy path, a duration that the profiler under-reports for these specific kernels, or dims that over-state the tensor -- and this cycle distinguishes none of them. What it establishes is that the three terms cannot all be right, which was not established before and which every lever in this campaign depends on. I PREDICTED REFUTED AND THE MEASUREMENT WENT THE OTHER WAY, at more than six times the threshold. My registered basis was that the large copies would be small enough for cache to explain -- I put the break-even at about 384 MiB and expected the trace's copies to sit under it. They are 1 GiB, nearly three times that size, and I had the trace on disk when I wrote the prediction. That is a guess where a lookup was available, which is the failure my own standing rule names, and the second cycle running where my reasoning was wrong: cycle 46 got the direction right for the wrong reason, this one got the direction wrong outright. Both generative, both mine. NOTHING IS RESTATED. No lever moves on this cycle. The elementwise lever stays 9,115.4, the optimizer 10,234.0, the stack 36,209.5 of 54,586.7. A finding that three terms cannot all be right does not tell me which figure to change, and changing one before knowing would be worse than leaving them.

Limitations, and what this does not close
READ THE HYPOTHESIS ID AGAINST THE VERDICT BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. This cycle inherited cycle 47's successor id, which asserts that the over-peak copies impeach the REFERENCE RATE and not the byte count. The criterion frozen at PREDICT is the opposite proposition -- SUPPORTED means the recorded operands justify a smaller byte figure, so the BYTE MODEL is the guilty term -- and the loop rules on the frozen criterion, not on the label. So this SUPPORTED REFUTES the sentence the id names. The id stands unedited because it is what cycle 47 actually conjectured and rewriting it would erase the conjecture that this cycle overturned, but nothing downstream may read the id as the finding. THE CORRECTION IS 4/3 AND IT IS A DTYPE-WIDTH RATIO, NOT A TRAVERSAL RATIO. bytes_corrected charges a movement op TRUE_TRAVERSALS[op] * max(sizes) -- twice the LARGEST operand for a copy. That is exact for a same-dtype copy, where source and destination are the same size. It over-charges whenever the operands differ, which is exactly what a converting copy is, and the over-charge is 2*max/(max+min) = 2*4/(4+2) = 4/3 for an fp32/bf16 pair. CYCLE 47'S MAGNITUDE ARGUMENT FOUND THE RIGHT NUMBER AND THE WRONG MECHANISM, AND THAT IS THE GENERALISABLE PART. It reasoned that bringing 1240.7 under 1084.2 needs 1.144, that a traversal miscount is wrong by a whole traversal -- it listed 2x, 1.5x and 1.33x explicitly -- and that no traversal arithmetic yields 14.4%, therefore the bytes were probably innocent. The available correction turned out to be 1.333, one of the three ratios it named, arriving by a mechanism it had not considered: operand WIDTH, not traversal COUNT. The inference "the required factor is not a ratio my mechanism produces, so my mechanism is not the culprit" silently assumes the mechanism list is complete. A required factor of 1.144 is a LOWER BOUND on the correction needed, never a target to match, and 1.333 clears it with room. I PREDICTED REFUTED AND I WAS WRONG FOR THE THIRD CYCLE RUNNING, AND THIS ONE IS THE WORST OF THE THREE. Cycle 46 was right for the wrong reason, cycle 47 was wrong outright from a guess where a lookup was available, and I wrote in this cycle's own prediction that its basis was "a SOURCE READ rather than a guess". The source read was accurate -- bytes_corrected does return 2 * max(sizes) -- and it answered what the model CHARGES. It said nothing about what the operands ARE, and I closed that gap with "I expect them to match" while the dims and dtypes sat in the same trace the instrument was about to read. A half-lookup presented as a lookup is worse than an admitted guess, because it carries the authority of the half that was checked. WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH, AND NO LEVER MOVES ON IT. The tested population is the eleven kernels that were already impossible -- it says nothing about the over-charge on the rest of the movement class, and nothing at all about the ARITHMETIC kernels, which is where most of the eliminable bytes live. The 13,703.8 us eliminable-bytes half of the elementwise lever is computed by this same formula and is therefore under suspicion, but suspicion is not a restatement: correcting it needs the class-wide share of unequal-operand kernels, which is the successor. Elementwise stays 9,115.4, optimizer 10,234.0, stack 36,209.5 of 54,586.7.

Limitations, and what this does not close
THE VERDICT IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE OVER-READ. 0.4519 against a 0.50 threshold is a refutation by four and a half points, not a demonstration that the ceiling is byte-shaped. Nearly half of it is contributed by kernels at the cap, where no byte figure and no launch figure enters at all. The honest summary is that the ceiling is a MIXTURE, roughly 45/55, and any later artifact that quotes this REFUTED as "the ceiling is a byte bound" is misquoting it. MY STATED DERIVATION WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AND THE INSTRUMENT PRINTED THE MISMATCH RATHER THAN SMOOTHING IT. I wrote before the run that a sized pure-movement kernel caps by construction and an arithmetic kernel with more than one sized operand can never cap. Observed: 4,700 of 4,704 pure-movement kernels capped, and 2,334 arithmetic kernels capped. The omission is that excess is CLAMPED at zero -- I wrote excess = dur - byte_time and the code is max(0, dur - byte_time). With the clamp there are THREE cap mechanisms, and attributing all 7,034 accounts for the composite exactly: (1) PURE MOVEMENT, SIZED -- 4,544 kernels, 7,107.2 us/step. bytes_eliminable returns bytes_corrected, so etime equals byte_time and rem equals dur identically. Capped by construction, as derived. The four pure-movement kernels below the cap are 1.3 us/step total and are floating-point rounding on that identity, not a finding. (2) UNSIZED -- 666 kernels, 110.2 us/step. nbytes is 0, so excess is the whole duration and etime is 0. The ceiling credits FULL REMOVAL of kernels whose operands never resolved: it says "these disappear entirely" about kernels it knows nothing about. 0.58% of the composite, named as a defect and deliberately NOT priced as a correction here, because pricing it would be a re-cut of a criterion after seeing numbers. (3) ARITHMETIC, SIZED, ABOVE THE REFERENCE RATE -- 1,824 kernels, 1,416.7 us/step. With excess clamped to zero, rem is etime alone, and rem >= dur means max(sizes) traversed once at 1008 GB/s takes longer than the kernel ran. These kernels achieve above the published peak on their eliminable bytes alone, which is cycle 47's above-peak population turning up in a third place. Cycle 48 explained the copies by operand width; nothing yet explains these, and their charge is the operand-aware sum+max fallback rather than the t*max form that was wrong. MY REGISTERED CALL WAS RIGHT, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN FIVE CYCLES, AND THE GAP I NAMED WAS THE ACTUAL GAP. I predicted REFUTED at 0.372, derived from cycle 47's published pure-movement device time, and wrote out that the instrument counts 7,034 capped kernels against that population's 4,548, so the extra kernels would raise the share by an amount I had not measured and could make me wrong. Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE REFUSALS BOUGHT, AND WHAT THEY DID NOT CLOSE. The instrument refused TWICE before it reported, at 0.6005 and then 0.8328 against a 0.85 coverage floor frozen before the run. Neither refusal was answered by lowering the floor. The first was cured by modelling backward contractions from their forward counterparts -- a backward records only its incoming-gradient shape -- and the second by deriving attention as the TWO contractions it actually performs (QK_T at (b*h, s, d, s) and AV at (b*h, s, s, d)) rather than charging it one averaged shape, since averaging two intensities either side of the ridge reports a boundary neither sits at. The second refusal also gained a residual listing: a refusal that does not name what it could not resolve ends the inquiry instead of directing it, and naming attention as the whole 11,970 us/step residual is what made the third run one patch away. THIS DOES NOT SAY THE GEMMS CURRENTLY RUN AT PEAK. Above the ridge means the arithmetic pipe is the binding constraint in a roofline sense, so a faster pipe CAN shorten the kernel. It is silent on whether these kernels are near their achievable rate today -- occupancy, tile quantisation, wave tails and epilogue cost are all unmeasured, and no occupancy counter exists anywhere in this campaign. So a 2x rate is an UPPER bound on what the precision route returns on this 91.3%, never a prediction of it. WHAT IT LEAVES OPEN FOR THE PRECISION ROUTE. Which of the two 2x carriers actually applies at each of these contractions is untested: FP8 is INCONCLUSIVE (row contractions measured faster than bf16 at forward 0.796 and dX 0.629 at m=4096, arm slower end to end on the wrapper and one dW event 2.72x its siblings) and FP16 ACCUMULATION has never been attempted at all and needs a cublasLt CUBLAS_COMPUTE_16F binding authored first. Nothing here adjudicates the #1413 loss bar for either. THE TAIL IS SMALL AND IS NOT ZERO. 0.8263 TF/step is traffic-bound, dominated by attention; mm at n=16 and n=1 and the two bmm rows are at 15.5, 1.0 and 0.7 FLOPs per byte and are rounding error in arithmetic while being real device time. aten::addmv_ at 10.7 us/step resolves no contraction at all.

The defect is mine and it is in the criterion, not in the measurement.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS RUN DID ESTABLISH, WHICH IS NOT NOTHING. (1) The binding exists: cuBLASLt is reachable by ctypes from the library torch itself loaded, and CUBLAS_COMPUTE_16F is ACCEPTED at all six measured contractions on this card. The carrier is not closed by the API. (2) The accumulator is GENUINELY HALF, proved numerically rather than assumed: the 16F path differs from the fp32-accumulate reference by 1.68e-03 relative L2 at every shape, against 0.0 to 3.3e-05 for the 32F path -- a separation of roughly two orders of magnitude, which is what a half accumulator looks like and what a silent fallback cannot produce. (3) Four of six shapes gain materially: 1.512x, 1.553x, 1.724x, 1.542x. THE FINDING THAT QUALIFIES THIS MORNING SHAPE RESULT, AND IT IS THE VALUABLE ONE. At m=2048 k=1024 n=1024 the 16F accumulator is ACTIVE -- its relative L2 is the same 1.68e-03 as everywhere else -- and it delivers 0.997x. No speedup at all. That contraction sits at 409.6 FLOPs per byte, two and a half times the 163.9 ridge, so the roofline classed it arithmetic-bound and predicted a faster pipe would shorten it. It did not. At m=1024 k=1024 n=256 (170.7 FLOPs per byte, just above the ridge) the gain is 1.114x. SO THE 91.3 PERCENT ARITHMETIC-BOUND SHARE IS AN UPPER BOUND ON WHAT THE PRECISION ROUTE CAN TOUCH, NOT A PREDICTION OF IT. Being above the ridge is necessary and is not sufficient; the registry row for that cycle already said a 2x rate is an upper bound and never a prediction, and this is the first measurement that shows the gap is real and large. THE INSTRUMENT DEFECT, CURED. The printed median took sorted()[n//2] on an even-length list, which is the UPPER middle value: it read 1.5419x where the median is 1.5270x. Favourable direction, again. Cured in place, with the arithmetic-versus-statistic requirement written into the docstring so the successor cannot inherit it. WHAT IS NOT CLOSED. The six shapes are the top of the census, not the census: they hold 33,138.4 of 71,674.3 us/step of eligible-site time, so 54 percent of it is unmeasured and the grouped-expert backward, the attention path and the 4,608-kernel m=1024 n=256 row are all outside the probe. Nothing here adjudicates the #1413 loss bar -- the relative L2 figures are reported with NO tolerance asserted, and a half accumulator over a full training step is a different question from one over a single contraction. And this is an ISOLATED-SHAPE probe: the FP8 arm had faster row contractions and was slower end to end.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED if the TIME-WEIGHTED speedup -- sum(us_i) / sum(us_i / s_i) over every timed b=1 contraction, weighted by its measured us/step from the shape census -- is below 1.50x. The 1.50x line is arithmetic and it is the SAME line the predecessor froze, now applied by the statistic that arithmetic requires: at 60 percent end-to-end utilisation the measured 9.2100 TF/step costs 92.9 ms in bf16, and applying a speedup s to the 0.9105 eligible share gives 92.9 x (0.0895 + 0.9105/s). At s equal to 1.50 that is 64.7 ms, still above the 51.2 ms the terminal allows; reaching 51.2 ms on this carrier ALONE would need s of about 1.97. So 1.50x is not a sufficiency line and is not claimed as one -- it is the line below which this carrier cannot be a material component of any composition that reaches the terminal, because below it the precision route buys less than the scheduling route already measured at 1.828x and would not be worth the loss-bar adjudication it costs. The criterion is frozen here, before the instrument runs, and will not be recut in either direction.

scripts/issue1945/fp16_accum_timeweighted.py
Evidence

Result in full
The frozen criterion was: REFUTED if the TIME-WEIGHTED speedup -- sum(us_i) / sum(us_i/s_i) over every timed b=1 contraction, weighted by its measured us/step -- is below 1.50x. MEASURED 1.3591x over 13 contractions holding 42,999.3 us/step, which is 94.4 percent of the derived shape census and 60.0 percent of the eligible-site total: 42,999.3 -> 31,639.2 us/step. The criterion is met on its refutation clause and the margin is not marginal. THE RESULT IS ROBUST TO THE THREE THINGS THAT COULD HAVE MADE IT AN ARTIFACT. (1) Estimator: the same composition on MEDIAN repeats rather than minimum repeats gives 1.3766x, also below the line. (2) Run-to-run: four independent runs of this question now give 1.3836x (the six-shape head), 1.3357x, 1.3474x and 1.3591x -- the composition is stable to about 0.04 while individual shapes swing by up to 0.2, which is the matched-run variation the evidence contract requires and it does not reach the line from any of them. (3) Accumulator identity: the 16F output differs from the 32F output of the same binding by 8.663e-04 to 9.383e-03 relative, every shape above the fp16 rounding unit of 4.883e-04, so a genuine half accumulator was running; the deliberate red, which substitutes a 32F accumulator into the 16F slot, produced a difference of exactly 0.000e+00 at 13 of 13 shapes and refused. WHAT IS REFUTED IS THE CARRIER AT THIS BAR, NOT THE PRECISION ROUTE. 1.3591x is a real speedup and it is below what the campaign already measured for the scheduling route (1.828x) without any numerical change and therefore without any loss-bar adjudication. On the criterion own arithmetic, 92.9 x (0.0895 + 0.9105/1.3591) is 70.5 ms against a 51.2 ms budget; reaching the budget on this carrier alone needs about 1.97x.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE. It does not close FP8, which stands INCONCLUSIVE with row contractions measured FASTER than bf16 (forward 0.796, dX 0.629 at m=4096) and an arm slower end to end on the wrapper plus one dW event 2.72x its siblings -- a different carrier with a different failure mode, and this result says nothing about it. It does not close the combination of FP16 accumulation with anything else: 1.3591x composed with the 1.828x scheduling floor is not 1.3591x, and no measurement here speaks to whether they contend. It does not close the four EXCLUDED batched contractions (2,532.3 us/step of attention and bmm) or the 26,142.1 us/step of MODELLED backward, together 40 percent of eligible-site time, none of it timed. And it adjudicates no loss bar: the relative differences above are reported as accumulator IDENTITY evidence with no tolerance asserted, so #1413 is untouched. THE FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE VERDICT. Five of thirteen contractions, holding 8,531.0 us/step or 19.8 percent of the timed set, gain nothing: m=2048 k=1024 n=1024 at 1.008x (409.6 FLOPs/byte), m=1024 k=32768 n=1024 at 0.970x (504.1), m=1024 k=256 n=1024 at 0.929x (170.7), and two small rows. Every one of them was classed ARITHMETIC-BOUND by the roofline, at up to three times the 163.9 ridge, and the half accumulator was demonstrably active in each. So the 91.3 percent arithmetic-bound share measured this morning is an UPPER BOUND on what any precision carrier can touch and is not a prediction of it -- the roofline over-predicts, and occupancy, tile quantisation and wave tails, none of which this campaign has ever measured, are the unexamined difference. THAT is the measurement the next cycle should be about, because it governs every remaining precision candidate rather than one carrier. FOUR INSTRUMENT DEFECTS, ALL FOUND BY ITS OWN GATES BEFORE ANY RATE WAS PUBLISHED, AND ONE OF THEM WAS AN OVER-CORRECTION OF ANOTHER. (a) The timing-agreement gate was symmetric and relative, so it refused on four small-output shapes where cuBLASLt was FASTER than torch by 5 to 11 us -- one dispatch, constant in absolute terms. Repaired directionally, which is stricter where a real defect would show. (b) That gate ran FIRST and pre-empted the deliberate red, so the red exited at a timing check instead of at the discriminator it exists to prove -- the identical defect the predecessor harness was repaired for one day earlier. Numerical gates now run first. (c) Each shape was timed ONCE; per-shape factors move by up to 0.21x on identical work, so timing is now 5 interleaved repeats with the spread reported and the estimator applied symmetrically to both arms. (d) The discriminator went through three forms and THE MIDDLE ONE WAS WORSE THAN THE FIRST: against torch fp16 it certified 12 of 13 shapes and failed at k=32768 on torch split-K ordering; against a common fp32 product it certified only 3 of 13, because both paths then pay the same 2.08e-04 fp16 output rounding as a common-mode term. The form that works removes the third party entirely -- 16F output against 32F output of the same binding, floor at the fp16 rounding unit 2**-11, derived from the format and not fitted to any observation.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED if machine fill, computed as CTAs/128 from the recorded m and n, fails to separate the two groups at ANY of the three canonical tiles 128x128, 128x64 and 64x64 -- that is, if the MINIMUM wave count among contractions gaining 1.40x or more is not strictly above the MAXIMUM wave count among those gaining under 1.10x. Requiring it at all three tiles is what stops the result being a property of a tile I guessed. ALSO REFUTED, and this is the clause that matters more, if any RIVAL quantity separates the same two groups just as cleanly: kernel duration, total FLOPs, output elements m*n, arithmetic intensity, contraction depth k, or census weight us/step, each asked in EITHER direction. A separation that a correlated quantity would also have produced explains nothing and cannot direct a measurement. The prior row above is exactly what an unguarded separation looks like when it is wrong. SUPPORTED requires BOTH: fill separates at every tile AND no rival separates at all. Anything else -- fill separates but a rival does too, or fill separates at two tiles of three -- is at most INCONCLUSIVE, and the verdict will say which clause failed. WHAT A SUPPORTED VERDICT WOULD AND WOULD NOT LICENSE. It would NOT prove the mechanism. Achieved occupancy, register pressure, shared-memory footprint, split-K decisions and the actually-selected tile are all unmeasured, and no counter for any of them exists anywhere in this campaign. It would license exactly one thing: that an occupancy measurement is the next instrument worth building, rather than one guess among several. That is a claim about where to point, and the criterion is frozen here, before the instrument runs, in both directions.

The deliberate red -- the measured speedups reassigned in reverse rank, so the labels scramble while every geometric quantity stays exactly as measured -- refuses at all three tiles. And the rival check, which is worth more than the separation: ARITHMETIC INTENSITY DOES NOT ORDER THESE GROUPS. Gaining spans 455.1-646.7 FLOPs/byte and the flat group includes 504.1 and 409.6, sitting INSIDE that range. Nor does kernel duration -- gaining spans 0.0586-0.435 ms and a flat shape sits at 0.409. WHY THIS IS NOT RULED SUPPORTED. One rival did separate -- output elements m*n -- so the first clause says REFUTED and the second says INCONCLUSIVE, and the frozen text does not decide between them. I also know which answer I would prefer, which is exactly why I am not taking it. m*n is not an independent rival: waves is ceil(m/T_m)*ceil(n/T_n)/SMs, a monotone function of m*n up to tile rounding, so it separates whenever fill does and its agreement is arithmetic rather than evidence. That is true, it was true before the instrument ran, and I only wrote it down AFTER the first red run showed the column fire. Excluding a rival from the frozen list after watching it fire is selecting the criterion to fit the data -- the identical move I refused in the FP16 cycle four hours ago and ruled INCONCLUSIVE for. SO: INCONCLUSIVE, on the ground that the frozen criterion is internally inconsistent, not that the measurement failed. The measurement did not fail. Every number above stands on its own and none of it is withdrawn. TWO INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT CRITERIA IN TWO CONSECUTIVE CYCLES IS A MECHANISM DEBT, NOT CARELESSNESS. Both have one shape: I wrote the criterion as PROSE, and prose admits two readings that only diverge once real numbers arrive. The cure is not more careful prose. It is that the instrument must COMPUTE and PRINT the verdict token from the frozen clauses, so the verdict is produced by code before I read any of it and cannot be adjudicated by interpretation afterwards. Applied to this instrument the same turn, so the successor inherits it.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS CYCLE ESTABLISHED, INDEPENDENT OF THE VERDICT WORD. 1. ARITHMETIC INTENSITY DOES NOT PREDICT PRECISION GAIN AT THESE SITES, AND OUTPUT SIZE DOES. That is the campaign-level consequence and it is larger than the carrier that exposed it. Every precision candidate still standing -- FP8 above all -- has been sized against the morning 91.3% arithmetic-bound share, a figure computed entirely from FLOPs per byte. This cycle says that share is an upper bound on reach and not a prediction of it, and names the variable that does the predicting instead. Any future FP8 ceiling quoting an intensity-derived eligible share without a machine-fill filter is quoting a number this cycle has already shown over-predicts. 2. THE BOUNDARY IS ONE FULL WAVE, AND IT IS TILE-INVARIANT. Flat maxes at exactly one wave and gaining minimums at exactly two, at every premised tile. The invariance is what makes it worth anything: the tile is the one premise this instrument cannot observe, and the result does not depend on it. WHAT IS NOT CLOSED. 3. THE MECHANISM IS NOT IDENTIFIED AND NO SHAPE ARITHMETIC CAN IDENTIFY IT. Machine fill and any other cost scaling with output size -- epilogue work per output element, store traffic, write-allocate behaviour -- are indistinguishable by construction here, because both are functions of m*n. This cycle narrows the question from why does the roofline over-predict to which output-size-scaling cost dominates, which is a smaller question and still an open one. 4. The rival check exists because of it and did its job -- it caught the one co-separating quantity. But a 2x2 that varies fill while HOLDING output size fixed is not constructible from shapes alone, so the confound that killed the prior reading is BOUNDED here rather than SEPARATED. 6. A shape deliberately placed between one and two waves would test it directly and none was run. 7. NOTHING HERE IS A GOVERNED MEASUREMENT. Derived CTA counts over a rate receipt from an isolated-shape probe. No model, no optimizer, no routing, no learning, no applied position, and no gate moves.

Which output size scaling cost dominates the sub wave contractionsdiagnostic on the cardThe two candidates predict different CURVE SHAPES, and a sweep across the boundary reads the shape directly.The frozen criterion said verbatim: 'REFUTED if no single jump between neighbouring points dominates -- specifically if the largest rise in speedup between adjacent m values is not more than 1.6x the second largest.REFUTED(1) THE m=2048 DIP, which is the only thing in the data that behaves like a boundary effect and which the frozen test could not see.
Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
The criterion is computed IN CODE by the instrument and printed as a verdict token before any table is read, and loop.py rule now refuses a ruling that disagrees with that token. This text states the same clauses in prose so a reader can check the implementation against them; where they disagree, that disagreement is itself the finding and neither is quietly adjusted. REFUTED if no single jump between neighbouring points dominates -- specifically if the largest rise in speedup between adjacent m values is not more than 1.6x the second largest. That is a smooth ramp, which is what a cost proportional to output elements predicts and what machine fill does not. It would mean machine fill separated the predecessor's two groups because it CORRELATES with output size, not because a full machine is what decides, and the predecessor's separation would keep its descriptive value and lose its mechanism. SUPPORTED if a jump dominates by that margin AND it sits at the one-wave boundary -- the wave count crosses 1.0 between the two m values bracketing it. INCONCLUSIVE if a jump dominates but is NOT at the boundary: something threshold-like is happening that this hypothesis does not describe. The transition is LOCATED from the data and not tested at a guessed point, which is what makes that third outcome reachable rather than collapsed into one of the other two. The 1.6x dominance margin is frozen here, before the run. It exists because 'the biggest jump' is a statistic that always returns something, and on a noisy curve it returns the noisiest pair; requiring it to beat the runner-up is what makes it a claim about a step rather than about a maximum. WHAT NO OUTCOME HERE LICENSES. This is not an occupancy measurement and no counter for occupancy, register pressure, shared memory or split-K exists anywhere in this campaign. A step at the boundary is strong evidence for the fill mechanism and is not a measurement of achieved occupancy. Isolated shapes at k=1024 n=1024; no model, optimizer, routing or learning, no applied position, and no gate moves either way.

scripts/issue1945/wave_boundary_sweep.py
Evidence
state\issue1945-receipts\wave-boundary-sweep-20260915T1730Z.json
Result in full
The frozen criterion said verbatim: 'REFUTED if no single jump between neighbouring points dominates -- specifically if the largest rise in speedup between adjacent m values is not more than 1.6x the second largest. That is a smooth ramp, which is what a cost proportional to output elements predicts and what machine fill does not. It would mean machine fill separated the predecessor's two groups because it CORRELATES with output size, not because a full machine is what decides, and the predecessor's separation would keep its descriptive value and lose its mechanism.' The largest jump is +0.236x (m=2048 -> m=2304) and the runner-up is +0.234x (m=1024 -> m=1536). 0.236 is 1.009x the runner-up, not 1.6x. The dominance clause is not met and the verdict is REFUTED by the clause as written. The instrument computed the same token from the same clauses before the table was printed. So machine fill is refuted as the MECHANISM. The predecessor's separation stands as a description -- CTAs/128 SMs does order the gaining and flat groups at all three tiles -- and loses its causal reading, because a quantity that is a monotone transform of output size will separate groups that output size separates. What survives is the weaker and better-supported statement: the gain from a faster accumulator rises with output elements, smoothly, across a 32-fold range of m. Boundaries, unchanged from the prediction: no occupancy, register, shared-memory or split-K quantity is measured here or anywhere in this campaign; isolated shapes at k=1024 n=1024; no model, optimizer, routing or learning; no applied position; no gate moves.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE. (1) THE m=2048 DIP, which is the only thing in the data that behaves like a boundary effect and which the frozen test could not see. The sweep reads 1.658x at m=1792 (0.88 waves), then 1.007x at m=2048 (exactly 1.00 waves), then 1.244x at m=2304 (1.12 waves). That is a fall of -0.651x and a recovery, sitting precisely at the one-wave boundary -- and the dominance test ranks RISES, so a sharp DIP is invisible to it by construction. The criterion was written expecting a step UP at the boundary and is silent about a notch DOWN at it. This is a defect of the criterion's shape, not of the measurement, and it is mine: the same class as the two self-contradicting criteria the verdict-token gate was built to catch, one layer over -- there the clauses disagreed with each other, here the clauses were internally consistent and did not cover the shape the data actually has. Note what the dip is NOT evidence for. It is a single point, the 16F timing spread over five repeats reaches 36%, and -0.651x is far outside that spread, so noise is an unlikely but unmeasured explanation -- no repeat-level statistic was frozen for it. The next cycle measures the neighbourhood of m=2048 densely (2016, 2032, 2048, 2064, 2080) with a pre-registered outlier criterion, and it must ask whether the dip tracks the CTA count crossing 128 or tracks m crossing a power of two, because at this tile those two coincide exactly and nothing here separates them. (2) PER-OUTPUT-ELEMENT COST IS NOT ESTABLISHED, only left standing. The ramp is consistent with epilogue work, store traffic and write-allocate behaviour, and equally consistent with anything else monotone in m. No byte of device-memory traffic has been measured in this campaign; the store-traffic reading is a hypothesis with no counter behind it. (3) ONE TILE, ONE SHAPE FAMILY. k=1024 and n=1024 are held fixed and the tile is assumed 128x128. The predecessor separated its groups at three tiles; this sweep tests one. A ramp at 128x128 does not establish a ramp at 64x64, where one wave arrives at a quarter of the m. (4) THE CURVE'S CEILING IS UNTESTED. Speedup is still climbing at m=16384 (1.571x) and the sweep stops there. Where it saturates, and at what value, is unmeasured -- and that value is the thing an eligibility estimate for the real contractions would need.

scripts/issue1945/which_kernel_actually_ran.py

Result in full
The frozen criterion said verbatim: 'INCONCLUSIVE if the two ANCHORS do not agree with each other, or if no 16F kernel is attributed at any of the three. If the selection is already varying across the neighbourhood, a difference at the dip carries no information, and this outcome is reachable rather than collapsed into one of the others.' The anchors do not agree. The 16F arm ran ampere_h16816gemm_128x128_ldg8_stages_64x3_nn at m=1792 and ampere_h16816gemm_256x128_ldg8_stages_32x3_nn at m=2304. The deliberate red held: with every kernel name replaced by one constant the discriminator reported REFUTED and not SUPPORTED, so the SUPPORTED path cannot be reached by an instrument that sees no differences. The dip therefore remains unexplained. What the run did settle is the tile premise, which it was designed to report under every outcome and which is recorded in the update rather than here, because it is not what this criterion adjudicates.
Limitations, and what this does not close
THE TILE PREMISE IS REFUTED, AND IT TAKES THE THREAD'S INTERPRETATION WITH IT. The audit reported a tile for 14 of 14 rows and they are not one tile. Four ran: 128x128, 128x64, 256x128 and 64x64. Both prior cycles computed every CTA count and every wave number from an assumed 128x128, so those numbers describe a kernel that did not run at most of these shapes. The predecessor's own docstring called the tile unobservable; it was observable in one run, and nobody had looked. THE LARGER FINDING, WHICH NEITHER CYCLE'S CRITERION WAS SHAPED TO SEE. The 32F and 16F arms do not run the same kernel with a different accumulator. They run DIFFERENT KERNELS, selected independently, with different tiles, at nearly every shape: m=1536 32F cutlass 64x64 16F ampere 128x128 m=1792 32F ampere 128x128 16F ampere 128x128 m=2016 32F ampere 128x128 16F ampere 128x64 m=2048 32F ampere 128x128 16F ampere 256x128 m=2080 32F cutlass 64x64 16F ampere 256x128 m=2304 32F cutlass 64x64 16F ampere 256x128 m=3072 32F ampere 128x64 16F ampere 128x128 So every 'speedup' in this thread -- the 13-contraction table, the 12-point sweep, the 1.3591x time-weighted figure that was REFUTED at the 1.50x bar, all of it -- is a ratio between two INDEPENDENTLY SELECTED KERNELS, not the effect of an accumulator width holding the kernel fixed. The heuristic is free to pick a different tile, a different stage count and a different kernel family for each compute type, and at these shapes it does. That does not make the measurements wrong. They measure exactly what a caller gets from cuBLASLt when it asks for one compute type versus the other, which is the operationally relevant quantity and is what a real contraction would receive. What it makes wrong is the MECHANISM language wrapped around them. 'A faster accumulator helps these shapes and not those' presumes one kernel running two ways. The honest statement is narrower and is what the receipts support: requesting CUBLAS_COMPUTE_16F yields a faster execution at these shapes than requesting CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F, by a margin that rises with output size, and part of that margin is the heuristic choosing a different kernel rather than the same kernel accumulating faster. WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE. (1) The m=2048 dip is still unexplained. Kernel identity does not account for it: m=2048, 2080 and 2304 all ran the same 256x128 16F kernel and the sweep read 1.007x, (unmeasured) and 1.244x across them. Something that is not the selected kernel varies there. The 32F arm DID change between 2048 (ampere 128x128) and 2304 (cutlass 64x64), so the denominator moved -- that is the next candidate and it was not tested here. (2) Whether the accumulator itself does anything is now an OPEN question, not a settled one. Separating it needs the kernel held fixed across the two compute types, which means driving the algorithm selection explicitly rather than taking the heuristic's default. That is a real instrument and it does not exist. (3) The four measured tiles are the tiles at k=n=1024 with half operands. Nothing here says what the real contractions select, and the census shapes were never run through this. (4) No rate is claimed. Every arm ran under a profiler and no duration from this run is quoted, including in its receipt. No occupancy, register, shared-memory or split-K counter exists anywhere in this campaign.

Are the largest unexplained sites a removable producer consumer pairdiagnostic, existing captureA producer/consumer pair has a signature a trace CAN carry, even though a trace carries no dataflow.The frozen criterion said verbatim: 'REFUTED if at EITHER shape fewer than 0.50 of the copies are matched by an add within the adjacency window.REFUTEDIt closes the specific proposition that a copy_/add_ pair at these two shapes is a producer feeding a single consumer whose intermediate a fusion could delete.
Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
The criterion is computed IN CODE and printed as a verdict token before any table is read, and loop.py rule refuses a ruling that disagrees with the token on a LANDED receipt. This states the same clauses in prose so a reader can check the implementation against them; where they disagree, that disagreement is the finding and neither is quietly adjusted. Shapes are [3072,1024] and [1024,3072]. Ops are aten::copy_ and aten::add_ (and their non-inplace forms). The adjacency window is 50 us, frozen here against a step of about 124 ms. REFUTED if at EITHER shape fewer than 0.50 of the copies are matched by an add within the adjacency window. The two ops are then not a producer/consumer pair, the byte-eliminating fusion does not apply at these sites, and the 7,017 us ceiling stands as the honest figure for this class. SUPPORTED if at BOTH shapes the copy and add counts agree within 5 percent AND at least 0.90 of copies are matched within the window. INCONCLUSIVE for anything between -- including counts that disagree while adjacency holds, and including either shape having no copy or no add event at all. A POSITIVE IS NOT REMOVABILITY, and this is registered before the run so a SUPPORTED cannot be read as more than it is. Removability is a property of the dataflow: whether the intermediate has exactly one consumer and whether that consumer can be expressed in the producer's epilogue. A trace records neither. SUPPORTED means the pairing signature is present and the source read is worth its cost; it does not license a ceiling, an arm, or a figure for what fusion would save. A DISAGREEMENT WITH THE PRIOR ROW'S POPULATION IS ITSELF THE FINDING. If no copy_/add_ event at these shapes carries the input dims this instrument filters on, the prior row derived its 6,433.2 us from a population this cannot find, and that is recorded as the result rather than cured by widening the filter until something matches. WHAT NO OUTCOME LICENSES. The trace was captured UNDER PROFILING INSTRUMENTATION, so no duration in it is a governed number and none is quoted as one. No occupancy, register-pressure, shared-memory or split-K counter exists in this build or anywhere in this campaign. One capture, one process, no matched-run variation. No model contract changes, no applied position, and no gate moves either way.
