The frozen criterion said verbatim: 'REFUTED if at EITHER shape fewer than 0.50 of the copies are matched by an add within the adjacency window. The two ops are then not a producer/consumer pair, the byte-eliminating fusion does not apply at these sites, and the 7,017 us ceiling stands as the honest figure for this class.' At [3072,1024]: 344 copies, 232 adds, 158 matched within 50 us = 0.459 of copies. That is below 0.50 and the clause fires. At [1024,3072]: 172 copies, 116 adds, 109 matched = 0.634, which is in the middle band, but the criterion is EITHER-shape and one shape is enough. The instrument computed REFUTED from the same clauses before the table was printed and the receipt carries the token. The counts also fail the balance half independently, and by the same ratio at both shapes: 344/232 and 172/116 are both 1.483. Copies outnumber adds by three to two at each site, so even if every add were matched the population cannot be one producer feeding one consumer. So the byte-eliminating fusion does not apply at these four sites, and the elementwise class ceiling stays 7,017.0 us/step of device time unexplained by bytes rather than rising to the 6,433.2 us the not-closed line left open.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE REFUTATION DOES AND DOES NOT CLOSE. It closes the specific proposition that a copy_/add_ pair at these two shapes is a producer feeding a single consumer whose intermediate a fusion could delete. It does NOT close the elementwise lever generally, and it does not say these sites are cheap. (1) THE 3:2 COUNT RATIO IS ITSELF UNEXPLAINED AND IS THE STRONGEST NEW THREAD. 344 copies to 232 adds at [3072,1024] and 172 to 116 at [1024,3072] -- identical ratio, exactly 1.483, at two different shapes. A coincidence of shape would not reproduce a ratio. Something structural issues three copies for every two adds at both sites and nothing here identifies it. The cheap next read is the source at the captured commit, not another trace pass. (2) MY OWN RED IS DEGENERATE ON THE ADJACENCY CLAUSE, AND I FOUND THAT ONLY BY READING BOTH LEGS. The red forces every timestamp to 0.0, which makes every add trivially adjacent to every copy -- so the red reported a HIGHER matched fraction (0.674) than the real run (0.459), and it held only because the count-balance clause failed. A red that raises the quantity it is meant to destroy is not exercising the clause it was written for. The adjacency clause therefore has no proven red, and the cure is a red that scatters timestamps rather than collapsing them. (3) THE DURATIONS HERE ARE NOT THE PRIOR ROW'S DURATIONS AND THE GAP IS NOT ADJUDICATED. This instrument sums the trace's `dur` field on aten:: operator events: 626.8 + 532.7 + 324.4 + 197.8 = 1,681.7 us per census window. The row that sent me here cites 6,433.2 us of DEVICE time at the same four sites. Those are different quantities read from the same trace -- host-side operator duration against attributed kernel time -- and which one belongs in a fusion estimate is not settled by this run. Neither is quoted as a governed number; the trace was captured under profiling instrumentation. (4) THE CENSUS WINDOW COUNT DOES NOT DIVIDE THE POPULATION. 344, 232, 172 and 116 are none of them multiples of the 6 windows passed on the command line, so the per-step figures above are averages over a population that is not uniform per step, or the window count is wrong. The counts are the measured quantity; the per-step division is a presentation and is the weaker half of the table. (5) NOTHING ABOUT REMOVABILITY WAS EVER REACHABLE HERE and the prediction said so before the run. A trace records no dataflow. This refutes a necessary condition, which is why a negative is decisive and a positive would not have been. NO GATE MOVES. Both halves hold exactly. 2,538 of 2,538 inner events are nested inside an outer event on the same thread, and 2,826 + 2,538 = 5,364, which is the predecessor count to the unit. A third event name containing the substring would have made the two-class model wrong and the instrument refuses on one; there is none. SO THE PUBLISHED RATIO WAS DOUBLE. On the engine-node unit the backward performs 235.5 gradient accumulations per optimizer step against 351 tensors carrying AdamW state: ratio 0.6709. Corrected, 235.5 gradient accumulations per step against 351 stepped tensors is a ratio of 0.6709: FEWER tensors receive a gradient than are stepped, which is the opposite of the published claim and is what a backward with no obvious waste looks like. THE MECHANISM DEBT, WHICH IS MINE. A substring test was used where an identity test was required, in an instrument whose whole output is a COUNT. The same class as taking a field by shape rather than by name. Any instrument whose headline is a count of events now matches event names exactly and prints the distinct matched names beside the count, so a fused class is visible in the output rather than invisible in the predicate.

(1) The 12 Optimizer.step# events partition into exactly two categories of exactly six: six 'user_annotation' on tid 38448 and six 'gpu_user_annotation' on tid 7. (2) Nearest-in-time pairing is a strict bijection -- six pairs, no host event claimed twice, no device event left over. (3) The largest intra-pair offset is 1,175.9 us and the smallest inter-pair gap is 462,003.3 us, a separation of 392.9x against a required 50x, so the two distributions are separable rather than merely ordered. (4) Both categories carry the identical name, 'Optimizer.step#AdamW.step', the only annotation name in the trace. SO THE TRACE CONTAINS SIX OPTIMIZER STEPS, NOT TWELVE. Twelve is one annotated scope rendered on two timelines, host and device, and counted once on each. BOTH REDS HELD ON DIFFERENT CLAUSES, which is what makes the four clauses four checks rather than one. Relabelling every device annotation to the host category reports REFUTED on clauses 1, 2 and 4. Pushing each device annotation out toward the next pair leaves clauses 1, 2 and 4 TRUE and refutes on clause 3 alone, at a separation of 1.7x against the required 50x -- so the separability clause is load-bearing on its own and is not carried by the others.
Limitations, and what this does not close
THIS CUTS AGAINST THE CYCLE I CLOSED AN HOUR AGO, AND IN THE DIRECTION THAT FLATTERS THE ORIGINAL CLAIM, SO IT GETS MORE SCRUTINY RATHER THAN LESS. Two double counts, in opposite places, very nearly cancelling: numerator -- 'AccumulateGrad' in name fused an autograd engine node with the op inside it: 2,826 + 2,538 = 5,364 where the engine-node count is 2,826. denominator -- twelve annotations fused a host scope with its device projection: six real steps where twelve were counted. Published: 5,364 / 12 = 447.0, ratio 1.2735. Corrected on both: 2,826 / 6 = 471.0, ratio 1.3419 against 351 stepped tensors. The withdrawn row's own frozen SUPPORTED line was 1.20, so the corrected figure CLEARS IT -- by a wider margin than the figure that was published. WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO: it does not re-rule that row. A verdict reinstated as a side effect of a different cycle's consequence block is a verdict nobody froze a criterion for. The re-ruling is the next cycle and it owes its own criterion before anything runs. WHAT IS STILL OPEN. (1) THE ANNOTATIONS ARE STEP MARKERS, NOT STEP SPANS. Zero of the 2,826 engine nodes fall inside any annotation interval; the host scope is about 1.7 ms and the device projection about 16 ms, while the inter-pair gap is about 462 ms. So dividing a whole-trace backward count by the marker count assumes the backward work is distributed evenly across the six steps. This cycle does not test that assumption, and a per-step figure built on it is a mean over the trace rather than a measurement of any one step. (2) THE PAIRING IS EVIDENCE, NOT A READING OF THE PROFILER. Nearest-in-time plus name identity plus 392.9x separation is strong, and it is still an inference about how the profiler renders one scope. Reading the emitter would settle it outright and was not done. (3) SIX STEPS IS A SMALL SAMPLE. Every per-step figure from this trace now rests on six intervals from one capture of one process under profiling instrumentation, with no matched-run variation. (4) NO RATE, NO CEILING, NO GATE. The step count is a count. Nothing here converts to microseconds and no gate moves. THE MECHANISM DEBT, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE TWICE IN ONE DAY. Both defects are a count over a name that denotes one thing and appears twice. The cure written after the first firing was 'match event names exactly and print the distinct matched names beside the count', and it would NOT have caught this one: the two annotation events carry the IDENTICAL name and differ only by category and thread. So the cure is widened: any instrument whose headline is a count of trace events prints the count broken down by (name, cat, tid) before it prints the total, so a class that is one thing rendered twice is visible in the output rather than invisible in the predicate.

Instrument
scripts/issue1945/is_the_inner_accumulategrad_record_conditional.py
Evidence
state\issue1945-receipts\inner-accumulategrad-record-conditionality.json
Result in full
All three frozen clauses hold at the installed torch header, and the verdict is SUPPORTED in the direction that costs me the margin. (1) The inner per-node record is opened inside Node::operator(), which is the single entry point every autograd node invocation passes through. (2) The only condition guarding it is C10_UNLIKELY(step_callbacks.has_value()), where step_callbacks comes from at::getStepCallbacksUnlessEmpty(at::RecordScope::BACKWARD_FUNCTION). No term in that guard refers to gradient state, accumulation, assignment or stealing -- the instrument searched for grad, accumulate, defined(), steal and assign and found none. (3) The record is opened BEFORE the call to apply(), so it cannot depend on anything apply does, including which of accumulateGrad's cases runs. THEREFORE THE BENIGN EXPLANATION IS REFUTED. A leaf whose .grad is assigned rather than accumulated still runs the same operator() and still emits the same record. Assignment versus accumulation cannot produce the 48-per-step gap. BOTH REDS HELD ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS: a mutated header whose guard tests inputs[0].grad().defined() reports REFUTED on clause 2 while clauses 1 and 3 still pass, so clause 2 is carrying its own weight; a mutated header with the emission deleted REFUSES rather than ruling either way, so the instrument cannot report an answer from a file that is not the emission site.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS DOES TO THE 471 FIGURE. It removes its explanation and leaves its arithmetic standing. The registry recorded the 48-per-step gap as CONSISTENT WITH leaves whose gradient is assigned rather than accumulated, and explicitly refused to call that demonstrated. It is now refuted: the emission is unconditional on the gradient path. So 48 nodes per step have an outer evaluate_function record and no record of their own, and nothing readable here says why. THE HONEST POSITION ON THE UNIT. At 471 engine nodes the ratio to 351 stepped tensors is 1.3419; at 423 inner ops it is 1.2051. Both clear the 1.20 line the predecessor row froze for itself, so the CONCLUSION -- more leaves receive gradients than have optimizer state -- survives either unit. The MARGIN does not: 0.14 versus 0.005. Every statement of that finding now carries both numbers, and the excess is 120 leaves per step at one unit and 72 at the other. WHAT IS STILL OPEN, NARROWED TO ONE THING. The wheel does not ship engine.cpp, so what makes the ENGINE emit an outer evaluate_function record for a node whose own record never appears is not readable on this host. The candidate mechanism worth testing next is the engine's exec_info needed_ path -- a node reached by the graph walk but not executed because nothing downstream needs it -- which would make the 48 nodes evaluated-but-skipped rather than executed, and would make the INNER op the right unit. That is a source question, not a trace question, and settling it needs either a torch source distribution or a fresh capture that distinguishes the two. WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE. It says nothing about which leaves are in the excess, nothing about cost -- still a count of graph work and not microseconds -- and nothing about any rate or ceiling. NO GATE MOVES.

All three frozen clauses hold, and the separation is not marginal. (1) The pairing is one-to-one: 2,538 of 2,538 inner events nest inside exactly one outer event on the same thread, and no outer contains two. 2,826 outers, 2,538 paired, 288 unpaired -- 48.0 per step, the same figure the count route produced by a different method. (2) The unpaired p90 is 1.600 us against a paired p10 of 15.100 us: the populations do not overlap at all. (3) The unpaired median is 1.300 us against half the paired median at 9.350 us -- a factor of 14.4 below the paired median of 18.700 us. THEREFORE THE 48 NODES PER STEP DO ESSENTIALLY NO WORK. A node costing 1.3 us is paying for the engine's graph walk and its own bookkeeping. It is not launching a kernel and it is not running an accumulation. The reading that would have kept 471 standing -- that these are real accumulations whose inner record merely failed to appear -- is dead: a real accumulation costs 18.7 us on this trace and these cost 1.3. BOTH REDS HELD ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS. Nesting a second inner event inside one outer makes the pairing ambiguous and the instrument REFUSES rather than ruling, so clause 1 is a real admissibility gate and not decoration. Redrawing the unpaired durations from the paired distribution makes the two populations coincide and the instrument reports REFUTED on clauses 2 and 3 with clause 1 still passing, so the separation clauses are carrying their own weight.
Limitations, and what this does not close
THE UNIT IS SETTLED AND IT IS NOT THE ONE I PUBLISHED. The 48 unpaired engine nodes per step are evaluated and skipped, not executed. So the count of leaves that actually RECEIVE a gradient per step is 423, not 471, and the ratio against 351 stepped tensors is 1.2051, not 1.3419. Both figures clear the 1.20 line the parent row froze, so the CONCLUSION stands unchanged -- more leaves receive gradients than have optimizer state -- but the margin is 0.005 and not 0.14, and the excess is 72 leaves per step and not 120. Every future statement of that finding uses 423, 1.2051 and 72. THAT MARGIN IS THIN ENOUGH TO BE ITS OWN FINDING. 1.2051 against a frozen 1.20 is a difference of about half a percent on a single capture with no matched-run variation. The parent row is not withdrawn -- its arithmetic is correct at both units and the direction of the conclusion is the same -- but a finding whose margin is 0.005 on one run is not a finding anyone should build a treatment on, and that is now said out loud rather than left to a reader to notice. WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE. It does not name exec_info. A near-zero-work outer event is consistent with the engine's needed_ skip path and with any other reason a node is walked and not run; the wheel ships no engine.cpp and no trace will distinguish them. The 1.3 us figure is also a host duration from a profiled capture, so it is evidence about relative work and not about cost. WHAT IS NOW WORTH ASKING, and is a different question from the one this answered: WHY 72. A skipped node is a node the graph walk reached whose output nothing downstream needs -- so the 72 leaves in the excess are receiving gradients that ARE consumed by something, while 48 per step are reached and discarded. Which 72, and whether they correspond to the require-grad-without-state placements in the checkpoint manifest, is still unknown: the trace carries shapes and not names. What the run DID establish, as a floor and never as the criterion's quantity: device time is 130,996.2 us per step by interval union; the AccumulateGrad nodes themselves carry 7,658.0 us per step across 2,538 correlated launches; the 72 unstepped leaves are 17.02 percent of the 423 accumulations, giving a prorated discarded floor of 1,303.49 us per step = 0.9951 percent of device time. Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE INCONCLUSIVE DOES NOT CLOSE. It does not say the discarded-work thread is small; it says THIS CAPTURE CANNOT WEIGH IT. The measured floor counts only the accumulation, prorated, and lands at 0.9951 percent of device time -- within a rounding error of the 1.0 percent line while excluding every upstream op on any exclusive path, which is the whole term the criterion was about. A floor that nearly clears the bar on its own while omitting the dominant term is the opposite of a reassuring result. It also does not close the hygiene question: 72 leaves per step carry gradients no optimizer state consumes, and that is a real defect in the parameter/optimizer reconciliation whatever its device cost turns out to be. THE SUCCESSOR IS AN INSTRUMENT QUESTION, NOT A TREATMENT. The blocker is a missing capture field, not a missing idea: torch profiler's with_stack or a record_function-tagged backward would let a backward op be attributed to the leaf it feeds, and profile_memory adds the allocation identity this trace lacks entirely. Before any capture is proposed, the standing constraints bind: a profiled number is not a governed number, the separation clause forbids composing across that boundary, and nothing about this justifies a GPU window until the field set is named and checked against what the profiler actually emits on this torch build. The cheap first leg is a source read of the profiler options, not a run. ALSO STILL OPEN from the parent thread and untouched here: why 48 leaves per step are reached by the backward walk and discarded, which 72 leaves are in the excess -- the trace carries shapes and not names -- and the reconciliation of 72 against the 74 require-grad-without-state placements in a different checkpoint at a different head, which is suggestive and is NOT claimed as a match. Evidence
state\issue1945-receipts\profiler-identity-capability-20260915T2117Z.json
Result in full
The criterion refutes when 'no option of THIS build causes any per-tensor identity key to be emitted for cpu_op events -- i.e. profile_memory writes only standalone memory records and never an Addr/Bytes arg on an op ... so no configuration of THIS build can attribute an op to a tensor'. That is what the build says. The Chrome-trace exporter's complete arg table sits in torch_cpu.dll at offset 225427380-225427800, bracketed on both sides by profiler_kineto.cpp: Module Hierarchy, Call stack, Python module id, Input Dims, Input Strides, Input type, Concrete Inputs, Fwd thread id, Sequence number, Record function id, Backend, Device Type, Device Id, Bytes, Total Allocated, Total Reserved. Addr is not in it, and there is no NUL-delimited Addr literal in any of the three compiled libraries; its 16 raw substring hits are AddrBackward0, GetProcAddress, PyCode_Addr2Line, TensorImplAddress_ and hostname prose, plus one bare Addr at 155866956 that is machine code rather than a string. The five keys that ARE present are volume keys: they record how many bytes were allocated on which device and what the allocator totals were, so two different tensors of the same size on the same device are indistinguishable in them. A size is not an identity, so there is nothing to join a memory record to an op BY. Half (a) therefore fails on the join key alone and the conjunction never reaches half (b), which independently holds (the capture site passes profile_memory=False and does not pass with_stack, with_modules or with_flops). The read is trusted because its method control passed 6 of 6 on keys the trace demonstrably carries -- Input Dims, Input Strides, Concrete Inputs, Ev Idx, Record function id, Sequence number -- so the absence is a fact about the build and not about the file set read.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE. (1) It refutes the CONFIGURATION explanation for this torch build's chrome-trace exporter only. It does not say the discarded-work question is unanswerable -- it says this route does not answer it, and it names three routes it leaves open. (2) The volume keys ARE real and profile_memory=True would emit them: Bytes, Device Type, Device Id, Total Allocated, Total Reserved on standalone memory records joinable to an op by timestamp and thread. That buys allocation VOLUME attribution per op window, which is a different and weaker quantity than tensor identity, and it is untested whether volume alone separates the exclusive backward subgraph feeding the 72 unstepped leaves. Worth its own cycle before being dismissed -- a refuted thread is not a dead end. (3) torch.profiler is not the only instrument. The allocator's own history (torch.cuda.memory._record_memory_history) records addresses and stack frames, and is not the chrome-trace exporter, so this refutation says nothing about it. That is the strongest remaining route and it is the named successor. (4) with_stack/with_modules would emit Call stack, Module Hierarchy and Python module id -- all three literals are present in the same table -- but backward ops' stacks point into the autograd engine rather than user code, so they attribute to the engine node and not the tensor. Predicted, not measured. (5) Affordability is unmeasured for every route above. This trace's wall span is near 590,000 us against a governed step of 175,190 us, so it is already instrumentation-dominated, and the governed-run evidence contract forbids reading any number off a heavier capture as a governed one. (6) The read is of the INSTALLED build, torch 2.10.0+cu126; a different build may carry Addr, and the finding is scoped to this one. (7) METHOD DEFECT RECORDED, second in this cycle: the first pass returned a false REFUTED by reading only torch's Python sources when the exporter is compiled into torch_cpu.dll, and the corrected pass then returned a false SUPPORTED by counting a raw substring hit -- Bytes matched inside resizePrivateUse1Bytes and Addr matched AddrBackward0 -- as though presence in a 253 MB binary were presence in the exporter. Both were cured by the same instrument: a method control over keys the artifact demonstrably carries, and a region rule that requires a hit to sit among the profiler's own literals or source paths. The control also earned its keep a third time: requiring two sibling keys refused Ev Idx, a key the trace does carry, which is what exposed that short literals pool away from the main table and forced the source-path markers in. A rule that refuses a known-present key is too strict whatever it would have said about the absent ones. (8) The half-(b) deliberate red had to be rebuilt as a labelled counterfactual: once half (a) became genuinely false, that red printed REFUTED without ever executing the branch it exists to prove, which is a red passing for the wrong reason.

Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT SUPPORTED DOES NOT CLOSE. (1) The autograd half of the criterion is UNMEASURED. A source read shows a frames field exists; it cannot show what those frames contain for an allocation made inside the backward pass. If they name the autograd engine rather than the op, this route buys an address with nothing useful to attribute it to -- the same defect that makes with_stack useless here. That is the named successor and it needs a real capture, not another read. (2) SUPPORTED says the attribution is RECORDABLE, never that it SUFFICES to partition the backward into the subgraph exclusive to the 72 unstepped leaves and the shared one. Recording an address per allocation and joining allocations to the ops that consumed them are different problems; the second is untested. (3) AFFORDABILITY IS UNMEASURED and the prior is bad: this trace already spans near 590,000 us against a governed step of 175,190 us, so it is instrumentation-dominated before any allocator history is switched on, and stack capture is the expensive mode. The governed-run evidence contract forbids reading a number off a heavier capture as a governed one, so whatever this route produces is diagnostic and earns no training-progress credit. (4) Scoped to torch 2.10.0+cu126 at this install path and to the five libraries read; a different build may differ. (5) The VOLUME-key route from the predecessor cycle is untested on its own terms -- per-op allocation volume joined by timestamp and thread is weaker than identity but was never tried, and the predecessor refuted the identity route, not that one. (6) METHOD DEFECT, and it is the third in three cycles, so it is recorded as a mechanism debt rather than an oversight. This instrument first returned SUPPORTED on sites that were every one of them a coincidence: "address" inside a kineto error sentence about address stability, "frames" inside a JIT interpreter assertion, "filename" and "name" inside aten schema strings, "addr" inside the pybind tensor-metadata accessors. It never opened torch_cuda.dll -- the library the allocator actually lives in. Three cures, all in the instrument: read that library; match only NUL or quote DELIMITED literals, which removes substring hits by construction; and strike the generic tokens (size, device, action, alloc, allocated, history, stream, line, name, snapshot, inactive) from the sibling set, because a discriminator built from words that appear everywhere in a 265 MB binary discriminates nothing. The previous cycle learned that the cluster rule could be too STRICT and widened it with region markers; this is the same rule failing in the opposite direction, and the fix was not a threshold but the vocabulary. (7) The verdict did not change when the evidence was corrected, which is the uncomfortable part and is why it is written down: a right answer reached through coincidental sites would have been landed, and only a neighbourhood inspection of my own favourable result caught it.

Result in full
The frozen criterion refutes "if no allocation in the snapshot carries any frame naming a generated autograd node -- the frames resolve only to allocator internals, engine entry points, or python". That is exactly what the capture produced, and the mechanism is blunter than the criterion anticipated. 60,000 device-trace entries, 444 segments, 34,986 frame-bearing records, 346,302 frame names, ZERO unresolved -- and ZERO carrying a "::" namespace. Every frame carries exactly (filename, line, name) and torch logged "record_context_cpp is not support on non-linux non-x86_64 platforms" during the run. So the C++ context recorder was REFUSED BY PLATFORM; this is not an unwind that stopped below the node frame, which a deeper limit might have fixed. No configuration of the recorder reaches the node on this Windows build. THE CAPTURE ITSELF SUCCEEDED, so the INCONCLUSIVE escape does not apply: the snapshot is 74,797,588 bytes with 60,000 trace entries, the method control the criterion named. WHAT THIS COSTS THE PREDECESSOR. The capability cycle ruled SUPPORTED on three readings that were each correct about the bytes: stacks="all" IS the default at cuda/memory.py:1002, CapturedTraceback IS in torch_python.dll, and the six generated node symbols ARE in torch_cpu.dll. Every one was silent about the platform, and the capability verdict it supported is now known to be unreachable at runtime here. Acceptance of a parameter is not execution of the feature it names -- which is the same lesson as symbol presence not being symbol resolution, one layer further out, and it is the reason this cycle was opened instead of the route being built on the source reads.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE, and the first item is larger than the refuted claim. (1) THE PYTHON FRAMES ARE THEMSELVES OP-LEVEL ATTRIBUTION, in ember's own vocabulary rather than in generated-symbol vocabulary. A 4 MiB backward allocation in this capture carries reduce_weight_gradient at ember_v0_document_reduction.py:93, backward at :119, and apply at torch/autograd/function.py:317. The top frame names across the capture are run, forward, main, step, _resident_documents_forward, _resident_segment, _eager, apply, backward, rotate_three_axis, reduce_weight_gradient; the top files are ember_v0_decoder.py (79,196 frames), ember_v0_capture.py (38,868), ember_v0_document_reduction.py (14,945), ember_v0_residency.py (13,221). For the open question -- WHICH backward work feeds the 72 leaves that require grad with no optimizer state -- a source file and line is arguably a better key than an autograd node name, because the leaves are named in ember source and not in torch's generated symbols. That is the successor hypothesis and it is registered as such, NOT as a rescue of this one: the criterion asked about node names and node names are absent. (2) NOT CLOSED FOR LINUX. The refusal is explicitly platform-conditional and this campaign is single-4090 on Windows, so the route is closed HERE, not in general. No portability claim is made and none is needed. (3) AFFORDABILITY IS NOW PARTLY MEASURED and it is cheap, which was not expected. Six measured steps ran at 0.372-0.493 s wall against a governed 0.2265 s p90 step, with the recorder on and 60,000 entries retained -- roughly 1.6-2.2x, not the order-of-magnitude the C++ unwind would have cost. The python recorder is affordable enough to use as an instrument. This is DIAGNOSTIC per the governed-run evidence contract: no number here is governed and the run earns no training-progress credit. (4) THE SEGMENT SIDE IS THIN. Only 12 of 444 segment blocks carried frames, so a live-block analysis has far less to work with than the trace; anything built on block provenance needs its own check. METHOD NOTE, and it is the reason this verdict is worth anything. TWO OF THE THREE DELIBERATE REDS WERE NO-OPS against this capture: --prove-red-no-nodes strips node names from a snapshot with none, and --prove-red-single-node collapses node names that do not exist. Both printed REFUTED and both would have printed REFUTED with the flag absent, so counting them would have been counting the real result three times. Only --prove-red-unresolved discriminated (0 -> 346,302 unresolved, clause 2 firing ahead of clause 1 on otherwise-clean data). That exposed the real gap: nothing showed the analyzer COULD return SUPPORTED, and an instrument with one reachable verdict carries no information when it refutes. So a synthetic positive control was built in the real snapshot's exact shape -- 500 real entries with native C++ node frames injected above their real python frames -- and against it the analyzer returns SUPPORTED (5 distinct nodes, 1,500 native frames), --prove-red-no-nodes turns it REFUTED with the 1,500 native frames still present, --prove-red-single-node turns it REFUTED at 1 distinct node, and --prove-red-unresolved turns it REFUTED at 100% unresolved. All four verdicts reachable, each clause firing on its own with the others left satisfiable. The synthetic snapshot is labelled SYNTHETIC in its own head and is a test of the RULER, never an observation about this build.

Joining the partition to the leaf set is a further cycle and the harder half. (2) THE VERDICT IS GRANULARITY-DEPENDENT AND THE DEPENDENCE WAS MEASURED, not assumed. That last failure is about the threshold and not the attribution: there are only 4 ember model files in the backward path, so a 5-key floor cannot be met at file granularity by construction. A second capture has not been taken and no repeatability claim is made. Checked directly -- the last frame of a tagged record is apply (3,118) or <module> (882), both outermost, and the first frames are call, reduce_weight_gradient, backward. Had the order been reversed the top key would have been one entry point at near 100%, which is also the shape the dominance clause would have caught. METHOD NOTE, and it is the finding I expect to reuse. THE SAME DEFECT FIRED THREE TIMES IN ONE CYCLE: a red or fixture that trips an EARLIER clause than the one it targets, refuses for the wrong reason, and reports success. First the no-ember fixture carried an ember path in its own backward marker and refused on dominance. Then the dominance fixture had 2 distinct keys and refused on few-keys. Then --prove-red-dominant collapsed every real key to one and again refused on few-keys. All three printed REFUTED, all three looked correct, and only the distinct-REASONS assertion in the self-test separated them. General form: with ordered clauses, a red must SATISFY every clause above the one it targets, and the check is that the reasons differ, never that the verdicts do.

SUPPORTED against the frozen criterion, and the source says it plainly. ember_v0_document_reduction.py:93-94 at head 20338ddc are the body of reduce_weight_gradient, whose loop header at line 92 is "for start, end in document_spans(lengths, order)". Line 93 allocates one partial per DOCUMENT -- upstream[start:end].transpose(0, 1) @ values[start:end], one document dY^T @ X at the weight shape -- and line 94 allocates the running total of the same shape. Neither loop in the enclosing chain iterates over parameters, gradients or leaves, so the refutation clause did not fire; both lines bind the result of an out-of-place operator, so the second clause did not fire either. The three distinct allocation sizes the census found are therefore three WEIGHT SHAPES reduced through this function, not three leaf classes, and the 255.3 allocations per step are documents times reduced linears -- a quantity with no leaf term in it at all. INSTRUMENT DEFECT, found by running it and repaired once: the first allocation detector listed named constructors only and classed "a.transpose(0,1) @ b" and "total + partial" as non-allocating, which would have refuted on clause 2 while looking at two expressions that each allocate an output tensor. The repair adds out-of-place operators whose result is bound to a name -- a fact about torch, not about this file -- and the deliberate red still fires on an import line under the wider detector.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE, AND WHAT IT OPENS. (1) It closes the ALLOCATION route for naming individual leaves at the reduction: this site cannot carry a per-leaf key at any granularity, size or volume, because it never allocates per leaf. It does NOT close the 72-unstepped-leaf question, which now has to be answered structurally from the parameter registry or from a non-allocation key, not from allocator provenance. (2) It speaks only for this function. The other 17 backward-tagged sites in the census, ember_v0_decoder.py:94 among them with 8 distinct sizes, were not read. (3) THE MORE INTERESTING HALF is that this is a SERIAL PER-DOCUMENT GEMM accumulation: one matmul and one add per document per reduced linear, each allocating, which is precisely the kernel-count regime of factor-table row 1. A batched or grouped reduction would collapse those launches. It is NOT a free win and must not be filed as one -- the function docstring states that nothing is promoted to a wider accumulator because matching the reference means matching its rounding, and the reduction ORDER is an argument, so any batching is contract-touching and owes an equivalence proof before it is a candidate. It is named as the successor, unranked and unsized: no ceiling is asserted here, because no measurement of this function device time exists. (4) Nothing here is device time or a governed quantity. The frozen criterion reads REFUTED IF the achieved rate on the causal convention comes back BELOW 0.75 of the published 165.2 TF/s. Every variant the instrument can defend refutes the hypothesis, so this is not a convention artefact. The explanation under test was that the class was unadjudicated for a measurement-convention reason rather than a performance one: the convention hole was real and is now closed, and the conclusion drawn from it was wrong.
Limitations, and what this does not close
The refutation does not close the thread, it relocates it. Two things the measurement itself hands over. (1) The backward is 9,479.3 us against a forward of 2,491.1 us, a 3.8x TIME ratio against a 2.0-2.5x FLOP ratio, so the two directions are not equally far from their references and the backward holds most of the 12.0 ms. (2) The trace records the kernel population as fmha_cutlassF/B_bf16_aligned_64x64_..._sm80 -- PyTorch memory-efficient cutlass at Ampere tiles -- which is an observation about WHICH backend ran on an Ada card and not yet a claim that any other backend is available or faster here. The successor asks why the backward specifically sits at 0.35, and it must be measured rather than argued: whether the backend is a choice or the only one this build offers is a source-and-runtime question, not an inference from a rate. Also still open and NOT closed by this refutation: the convention itself, which is now settled and reusable for any later attention measurement on this campaign.

Both refuting branches require flash to be present and neither obtains, so the dispatcher never had it to choose from and the memory-efficient cutlass path was the only one reachable -- which is what the census sees: one symbol, every call, forward and backward. Tested against tensors built the way _document_sdpa builds them, stride 1048576/64/1024/1 and non-contiguous, because stride eligibility is one of the things flash checks. Note for the successor rather than for this verdict: flash_sdp_enabled() returns TRUE while the kernels are absent, so that flag reports the preference and not the capability.
Limitations, and what this does not close
SUPPORTED, and the support hands over a sharper question than the one asked. can_use_cudnn_attention returns TRUE for this exact signature and cudnn_sdp_enabled() is true, yet the dispatcher still took the cutlass memory-efficient path -- one symbol, every call. Priority order, a later runtime check, or the cutlass path genuinely winning selection are all consistent with what was observed and the probe cannot distinguish them. That is measurable and it is cheaper to measure than to argue. What this does NOT license: it does not say a flash-enabled build or the cudnn backend would be faster at this geometry. Nothing was compared and nothing was timed, and asserting a saving would be a claim about what a change collects. It also does not make swapping the torch build a treatment -- the build is part of the declared execution environment every governed receipt binds to, so changing it carries its own equivalence question rather than being a kernel swap.

So one of two things is true and they have opposite consequences: either the bytes are largely cache-resident, in which case DRAM is the wrong roofline and the block has real unmeasured distance against an L2 roofline; or the byte figure derived from recorded input dims is wrong, in which case every bandwidth number in the census inherits the error. Either answer is load-bearing and the discriminator reads a trace already on disk.

The overlap row (overlap-bandwidth-bound-with-compute-bound-work) is on the registry as INCONCLUSIVE precisely because it lacked that split, and it now has it. Its premise -- that these two populations run STRICTLY IN SERIES on the device today -- has never been measured; it has only been assumed from the shape of a single-stream step. I am deliberately not composing them here: asserting what one act would collect is a claim about the act, and that is the class of statement my own record says I get wrong.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
Computing the INTERVAL UNION of the compute-bound population's kernel intervals and the interval union of the bandwidth-bound population's kernel intervals, then the union of the two together, shows the combined union is materially SMALLER than the sum of the two separate unions -- concretely, that the overlapping device time between the two populations exceeds 15 percent of the smaller population's union. Above that, the populations already run concurrently to a material degree, strict serialization is NOT the state of this step, and factor-table row 3's premise is wrong as written.

Limitations, and what this does not close
NOT CLOSED, and this list is what stands between the measurement and any factor. (a) WHICH PAIRS ARE DEPENDENCY-FREE is unmeasured. Serialization on one stream is not evidence the work COULD run concurrently; a strict data dependency serialises regardless of stream count. The step's dependency graph has not been extracted. (b) WHAT CONCURRENT EXECUTION WOULD COST is unmeasured. A max() of two unions assumes co-resident kernels do not contend, and a bandwidth-bound kernel sharing SMs and the memory system with a GEMM contends for both, so the real cost sits somewhere between max() and the sum. (c) THE 35,958.4 us other CLASS sits in neither population -- larger than the bandwidth-bound union minus its top member -- and which side it lands on changes any composition materially. (d) One profiled trace, one geometry, no matched-run variation. NO CEILING IS COMPOSED AND NONE MAY BE QUOTED FROM THESE NUMBERS. SUCCESSOR: (a) is the cheapest of the three and gates the other two, so it goes first. The question is how much of the serialized timeline is serialized by DEPENDENCY versus by STREAM ORDER ALONE. The trace carries the cpu_op nesting and the launch order; a first pass can bound it by asking, for adjacent kernel pairs drawn from the two populations, whether the later kernel's innermost op consumes a tensor the earlier one produced. That is a lower bound on dependency-free adjacency, not the full graph, and it is analysis-only.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
For adjacent kernel pairs in issue order where one kernel belongs to the compute-bound population and the other to the bandwidth-bound population, joining each kernel to its innermost enclosing cpu_op and comparing the later op's recorded input identity against the earlier op's output shows that MORE THAN 85 percent of such adjacent cross-population pairs are producer-consumer related. Above 85 percent the chain is the model's own dataflow, the serialization is forced rather than scheduled, and no rescheduling treatment reaches it -- which would close the concurrency direction as an arm.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
The optimizer block achieved bandwidth -- computed as the AdamW read/write byte set over the TRAINABLE parameter inventory at the measured head, divided by the measured 16,817.8 us per step -- comes out BELOW 50 percent of the 1008 GB/s published DRAM rate. That refutes the bandwidth-bound reading: the block would then not be limited by its own byte count, every byte-reduction treatment would be aimed at the wrong quantity, and the cost would have to be attributed to launch structure, chunking or occupancy instead. SUPPORTED IF at or above 50 percent: the block is bandwidth-bound at its own byte count, fewer bytes is the only lever on it, and the inventory names which state tensors carry them. THE 1008 GB/s FIGURE IS A PUBLISHED SPECIFICATION USED TO EXPRESS A FRACTION, never a measured ceiling and never an impossibility premise -- the same census already recorded three classes reporting ABOVE it on L2 residency, so a result above 100 percent is a finding about residency and not a contradiction. The byte count will be a LOWER BOUND (each tensor in the read/write set counted once, no cache reuse modelled), so the computed rate is at or BELOW the true one, which is conservative for the refutation direction and must be stated as such in the ruling.

REFUTED against the frozen criterion, which reads: "The optimizer block achieved bandwidth -- computed as the AdamW read/write byte set over the TRAINABLE parameter inventory at the measured head, divided by the measured 16,817.8 us per step -- comes out BELOW 50 percent of the 1008 GB/s published DRAM rate." It comes out at 39.15 percent (394.6 GB/s), and at 29.80 percent at the other end of the bracket below, so it is refuted across every population the traced steps could have stepped. THE BYTE MODEL IS CHECKED AGAINST THE COMPILED KERNEL RATHER THAN ASSERTED. The measured symbol is multi_tensor_apply_kernel<FusedOptimizerTensorListMetadata<4>, FusedAdamMathFunctor<BFloat16, 4, ADAM_MODE 1, false>>, and the instrument REFUSES if any of ILi4EE (FOUR tensor lists: param, grad, exp_avg, exp_avg_sq and nothing else), BFloat16, ADAM_MODE 1 (decoupled weight decay) or Lb0 (amsgrad false) is absent. So seven traversals at 2 bytes = 14 bytes per stepped element is the compiled fact, not my reasoning about AdamW. The checkpoint corroborates it: master_weights is None, params are bfloat16 across all 26 objects, and exp_avg/exp_avg_sq are bfloat16 across all 351 state entries. THE VERDICT TURNED ON WHICH POPULATION THE KERNEL WALKS, AND THE CONVENIENT ANSWER WOULD HAVE RULED THE OTHER WAY. Three candidates: 474,030,080 elements that actually carry AdamW state (39.15 percent), 701,964,288 declared trainable by the runner model receipt (57.97 percent, which is SUPPORTED), and 3,082,539,008 in the whole optimizer param_group (254.57 percent). The criterion says "the TRAINABLE parameter inventory", which literally points at the declared figure -- the one from the easiest source that yields the more interesting result. It was not taken. THE DISCRIMINATOR COMES FROM THE TRACE, NOT FROM A DECLARATION. multi_tensor_apply walks the tensor list accumulating 64 Ki-element chunks and launches whenever it reaches 36 tensors or 320 chunks (MultiTensorApply.cuh, depth 4). Simulating that greedy walk predicts the launch count each population would produce, and the trace MEASURED that count at 21.3 per step: the stepped set predicts 23 (error 1.7), the declared set 34 (error 12.7), the param_group 148. And the 1.7 residual is explained rather than waved at -- routing is data-dependent and the capture is 6 steps against an hour of updates, so core-only predicts 13, core+expert0 predicts 18, core+experts0and1 predicts 23, and the measured 21.3 sits between the last two. That brackets the traced population at 360,783,872 to 474,030,080 elements and the achieved bandwidth at 29.80 to 39.15 percent. WHAT THE REFUTATION LICENSES: nothing byte-shaped. At 394.6 GB/s roughly 60 percent of the card streaming capacity is unused during the block, so lower-precision moments, a fewer-traversal state layout, and fusing the update into the backward are all aimed at a quantity that is not the constraint. The moments are ALREADY bf16, which is part of the answer: the obvious byte lever had been pulled before the block was ever examined. WHAT IT DOES NOT LICENSE: the reverse claim. Not-bandwidth-bound is not launch-bound. Three candidates survive and this instrument discriminates none of them. THE ONE ASSUMED TERM CUTS AGAINST THE RULING AND IS STATED FOR THAT REASON. A gradient is not stored in a checkpoint, so its dtype is taken equal to the parameter dtype. Were gradients fp32 the count would be 18 bytes per element instead of 14 and the stepped population would land at 50.3 percent -- at the boundary. The single BFloat16 functor instantiation in the measured symbol is the evidence against that, since a mixed-precision variant instantiates differently, but no artifact states the gradient dtype outright. Separately, no cache reuse is modelled, so the byte count is a LOWER BOUND and the true rate is at or above the computed one -- which is the direction that would weaken this refutation, and is why the 50 percent bar is cleared by 11 points rather than by one.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE, named while the reasoning is fresh rather than later under pressure to reopen a route. (a) IT NAMES NO MECHANISM. Three survive: launch structure across 21.3 launches per step; the chunk tail, since 351 tensors of very unequal size are packed into fixed 36-tensor / 320-chunk batches and the last batch of each launch is partial; and occupancy, for which this trace carries no counter at all. Discriminating them is the next cycle. (b) A BYTE-REDUCTION TREATMENT IS REFUSED AT THIS GEOMETRY AND THIS POPULATION ONLY. If the stepped population grew by an order of magnitude -- more experts receiving gradients, a wider trainable set -- the same block would move toward the streaming regime and the byte lever would come back. This row does not close the byte route for a different configuration. (c) IT MEASURES NO BYTES. Every figure is derived from an inventory, not read from a device-memory traffic counter. The governed-run evidence contract requires measured traffic reported separately from estimated traffic, and no measured traffic exists for this trace. (d) THE GRADIENT DTYPE IS UNSTATED BY ANY ARTIFACT. If it is fp32 the ruling sits at the boundary rather than clear of it. Reading it off a live step would settle it cheaply. (e) IT SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHETHER THE OPTIMIZER CAN BE OVERLAPPED. The factor-table row-3 proposal -- per-layer optimizer on a second stream as backward finishes each layer -- is a concurrency treatment, not a byte treatment, and this refutation neither supports nor weakens it. Cycle 8 restated composition already carries that 16.8 ms on the overlappable side under an assumption it explicitly flagged as the strongest in the construction. Reported because it is what SIZES the population and therefore decides this verdict. It is NOT a ruling that anything is misconfigured and this artifact did not investigate it; whether the router and the remaining active experts are meant to be receiving gradients at this head is a separate question. Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
The per-launch grid geometry of the 21.3 multi_tensor_apply launches, read from the trace kernel events, shows a MEDIAN grid of AT LEAST 288 blocks -- 90 percent of the 320-block cap. REFUTED against the frozen criterion, which reads: "the per-launch grid geometry of the 21.3 multi_tensor_apply launches, read from the trace kernel events, shows a MEDIAN grid of AT LEAST 288 blocks -- 90 percent of the 320-block cap." The median is 320 -- EXACTLY the cap -- so the criterion is refuted with 32 blocks to spare, not marginally. THE CAP ATTRIBUTION IS UNANIMOUS AND IT IS THE FINDING. Grid distribution over 128 launches: 320 blocks x122, 222 x4, 46 x2. Holding out the six terminal launches -- one per step, which end on list exhaustion rather than on any cap -- 122 OF 122 INTERIOR LAUNCHES ENDED ON THE 320-BLOCK CHUNK CAP AND NOT ONE ENDED ON THE 36-TENSOR METADATA CAP. Candidate (1), the tensor-cap reading, is dead. THE INVENTORY SAYS THE SAME THING INDEPENDENTLY. Simulating the greedy multi_tensor_apply walk over the cycle-9 stepped set and recording WHICH cap terminated each launch gives 22 chunk-cap terminations plus one exhaustion and ZERO tensor-cap terminations; core+expert0 gives 17 and one, also zero. The reason is the size distribution: 351 tensors over 7,342 chunks is 20.9 chunks per tensor, so 36 tensors is about 750 chunks and the block cap is reached first almost every time, and the largest single tensor is 33,554,432 elements = 512 chunks, which alone exceeds the 320-block cap. The tensor cap could still have bound had the 110 one-chunk tensors been contiguous in list order; they are not. CANDIDATE (2), THE CHUNK TAIL, IS REFUTED AS A SIDE EFFECT BY ARITHMETIC THAT NEEDED NO NEW MEASUREMENT. Dispatched thread slots for the 351-tensor population are 7,342 chunks x 65,536 = 481,165,312 against 474,030,080 elements, so 7,135,232 slots do no work: 1.48 percent. For core+expert0 it is 1.94 percent. A mechanism worth under 2 percent cannot account for a block running at roughly a third of the card streaming rate. Those numbers were in cycle 9 inventory the whole time. A THIRD AND FINER ESTIMATE OF THE STEPPED POPULATION, WHICH TIGHTENS CYCLE 9 AND MOVES IT IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION, SO IT IS STATED AS RECHECKABLE ARITHMETIC. The sum of every launch grid is the dispatched chunk count: 6,670 per step. Each tensor contributes at most one partial final chunk, so 414,121,984 < elements <= 437,125,120, which is 34.20 to 36.10 percent of the published DRAM rate over the whole block -- INSIDE and tighter than cycle 9 published bracket of 29.80 to 39.15. It is the fourth independent estimate of this population to land in the same place: checkpoint state presence 474.0 M, simulated launch count 360.8 to 474.0 M, the governed hour routing distribution 439.1 M mean, and now the dispatched chunk count 414.1 to 437.1 M. THE CRITERION PREMISE ABOUT OCCUPANCY WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS PUBLISHED RATHER THAN QUIETLY ADJUSTED. The criterion says "this trace carries no occupancy counter". Every kernel event carries est. achieved occupancy percent, registers per thread, blocks per SM and warps per SM. It is CUPTI occupancy-calculator ESTIMATE from the launch configuration, not a sampled count of resident warps -- so the criterion is right that no MEASURED counter exists and wrong that nothing about occupancy is readable, and an available estimate is not withheld because the criterion did not anticipate it. 64 registers per thread on all 128 launches, 512-thread blocks, so 32,768 registers per block against the device 65,536 per SM = 2 resident blocks = 1,024 of 1,536 resident threads = 66.7 percent theoretical, and the trace reports a 67 percent median. The launch is REGISTER-LIMITED to two blocks per SM. Second observation from the same numbers: 128 SMs x 2 blocks = 256 resident against a 320-block grid, so a full launch is 1.25 waves and its second wave fills a quarter of the machine. WHAT THIS DOES NOT LICENSE. It does not confirm candidate (3). A kernel can run at 67 percent occupancy and be fine; occupancy bounds latency hiding and nothing here shows this kernel needs more of it. And the tempting composition is explicitly NOT made: 36 percent of DRAM at 67 percent occupancy invites "therefore about 54 percent at full occupancy", which assumes achieved bandwidth scales linearly with occupancy -- established by nothing, and exactly the shape of composition my own record says I get wrong. It is carried to cycle 11 as a hypothesis to falsify, never as a ceiling.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THE REFUTATION DOES NOT CLOSE, named while the reasoning is fresh. (a) IT DOES NOT RULE ON OCCUPANCY, candidate (3), which is now the only survivor. The evidence is a launch-CONFIGURATION estimate, not a sampled counter of resident warps. That (3) is the last candidate standing is an elimination, not a confirmation, and a kernel at 67 percent occupancy may be perfectly well fed. (b) THE TENSOR CAP IS REFUTED AT THIS POPULATION AND THIS LIST ORDER ONLY. It binds when many small tensors are contiguous in the list; here 110 of 351 tensors are single-chunk and they are interleaved with large ones. A different trainable set, a different parameter ordering, or a configuration with far more small tensors could make it bind, and this row does not close that. (c) THE CHUNK-TAIL REFUTATION BOUNDS THE TAIL SPECIFICALLY, at 1.48 percent of dispatched thread slots. It says nothing about threads that are dispatched and then idle for another reason -- a divergent branch, a stalled load, imbalance within a chunk. Those would be invisible to both the grid geometry and the slot arithmetic. (d) A REPACK OR A FLATTENED CONTIGUOUS UPDATE IS NOT FULLY CLOSED BY (b) AND (c) TOGETHER. What is closed is the REASON originally offered for it -- that the grid is small by construction, which it is not. A flattened update over one contiguous state buffer would still remove 21 launches per step and their metadata setup, and this cycle measured neither the per-launch fixed cost nor the metadata cost. Whether that is worth anything is unmeasured, not refuted. (e) IT MEASURES NO BYTES. Section 5 bounds are element counts from dispatched chunks times the cycle-9 byte model. No device-memory traffic counter exists for this trace and the governed-run evidence contract requires measured traffic reported separately from estimated. (f) THE 1.25-WAVE OBSERVATION IS ARITHMETIC FROM THE LAUNCH CONFIGURATION AND THE DEVICE PROPERTIES, not a measured scheduling behaviour. Whether the tail wave actually costs time depends on whether launches overlap, and the fused-AdamW launches in this trace were not checked for overlap with each other or with anything else. That check is cheap and was not run. (g) THE CENSUS-TO-TRACE BINDING HOLE FOUND IN THIS CYCLE OWN FIRST DRAFT IS CURED HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE. Every other instrument on this campaign that takes a trace and a census binds them by head and step count only, and would accept a census from a different capture at the same head. In this case the substitution would have been immaterial, which is why it was invisible; the class is not. (h) ONE TRACE, ONE GEOMETRY, ONE CHECKPOINT, under profiling instrumentation, with no matched-run variation.

Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF the ratio of achieved bandwidth (the higher-occupancy multi_tensor_apply class to the fused-AdamW class), each computed PER LAUNCH from that launch own grid and duration and then aggregated, EXCEEDS 1.55 -- that is, exceeds the 83:67 = 1.24 occupancy ratio by more than 25 percent. Either outcome means the comparison did not discriminate and the ruling says so rather than picking the convenient branch. Result in full
INCONCLUSIVE against the frozen criterion, taking the branch the criterion itself names as correct rather than a fallback: "INCONCLUSIVE IS THE CORRECT VERDICT, NOT A FALLBACK, IF the pure-store confound cannot be separated -- specifically if _foreach_add_ (read + write) disagrees in DIRECTION with _foreach_zero_ (pure store), or if its 4 launches per step are too few to distinguish its rate from noise at the sample size available. Either outcome means the comparison did not discriminate and the ruling says so rather than picking the convenient branch." THE NUMERIC BAR WAS CLEARED AND IT DOES NOT RULE. The bandwidth ratio is 2.3486 against a bar of 1.55 -- it exceeds the refutation threshold by 52 percent. The ratio is element-free: both classes dispatched exactly 40,020 chunks, so bw_zero/bw_adamw = (2/14) x (95,084.2/5,783.6) with no estimate of the stepped population entering it at all. In absolute terms and using cycle 10 chunk-count upper bound of 437,125,120 elements, fused AdamW is 386.2 GB/s (38.31 percent of published DRAM) at 67 percent occupancy and _foreach_zero_ is 907.0 GB/s (89.98 percent) at 83 percent. THE CONFOUND CONTROL MEASURED NOTHING, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE RULING. _foreach_add_ durations across an 18.3x span of work: grid 6 gives 1.472 to 1.536 us, grid 9 gives 1.504, grid 110 gives 1.504 to 2.016 us. The ranges OVERLAP and the median moves 1.03x for 18.3x the work, so the implied cost per chunk falls 18x as the grid grows -- the signature of a fixed floor (launch, timer granularity, tail), not a bandwidth measurement. The one control appointed to separate the pure-store confound therefore cannot say whether a read+write kernel at a different occupancy is fast or slow. WHY THE CONFOUND IS NOT A TECHNICALITY. _foreach_zero_ issues stores that retire fire-and-forget; fused AdamW issues four dependent load streams that must be waited on. A store kernel hides latency better REGARDLESS of resident warps, so a 2.35x advantage is exactly what the confound predicts with zero occupancy contribution. Ruling REFUTED would assert that occupancy is eliminated when the evidence is equally consistent with the entire excess being store/load asymmetry. THE INSTRUMENT CAN REACH THE OTHER BRANCH, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS VERDICT FALSIFIABLE. On a synthetic fixture identical in every respect except that its _foreach_add_ DOES resolve its rate (20x work span, non-overlapping durations), the same code returns REFUTED at ratio 2.3292. Without that green the INCONCLUSIVE would be the only outcome the code could produce and would prove nothing. A SUPPLEMENTARY CONTROL WAS FOUND, IS PUBLISHED, AND DELIBERATELY DOES NOT RULE. The trace carries vectorized_elementwise_kernel<4, CUDAFunctor_add<BFloat16>> -- a genuine read+write class at 100 percent occupancy whose element count is derivable from grid x block x 4. At its largest shape (32,768 blocks x 128 threads = 16,777,216 elements, 100 MB of traffic, 30 samples, 213.398 us median) it achieves 471.7 GB/s, which is 1.22x the fused-AdamW rate against an occupancy ratio of 1.49x -- BELOW proportional. It is not substituted for the frozen control because picking a discriminator after the numbers are in is choosing it to fit the answer, the same defect cycle 9 recorded about denominators. It is pre-registered for cycle 12 instead.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS CYCLE DOES NOT CLOSE, named while the reasoning is fresh. (a) CANDIDATE (3), OCCUPANCY, SURVIVES EXACTLY AS IT ENTERED. Three cycles have now failed to close it: cycle 9 left it standing, cycle 10 eliminated its rivals without confirming it, and cycle 11 could not separate it from the store/load confound. It is neither confirmed nor refuted and must not be reported as either. (b) THE 2.3486 RATIO IS A REAL MEASUREMENT AND IT IS NOT RETIRED. What is unavailable is its INTERPRETATION, because two mechanisms predict it equally well. A successor that separates them inherits this number rather than re-measuring it. (c) THE PRE-REGISTERED SUCCESSOR CONTROL IS NAMED: vectorized_elementwise_kernel<4, CUDAFunctor_add<BFloat16>> at 100 percent occupancy, 6 bytes per element, element count grid x block x 4. Its confound is the reverse of this cycle -- it is a DIFFERENT access pattern over a DIFFERENT tensor rather than the same list, so it trades the population identity that made this cycle ratio element-free for the read+write shape this cycle lacked. That trade must be stated in the next criterion BEFORE it runs. (d) THE DENOMINATOR OF THE WHOLE CAMPAIGN IS NOW IN QUESTION AND THIS ROW SAYS SO AGAINST INTEREST. Cycles 9 through 11 all read the optimizer block against a PUBLISHED 1008 GB/s spec figure. The supplementary control suggests this host, under this instrumentation, demonstrates roughly 472 GB/s on a read+write stream at full occupancy. If that holds, fused AdamW at 386.2 GB/s is 1.22x off a demonstrated rate rather than 2.6x off a spec sheet, and the "third of streaming rate" framing three cycles were built on shrinks to a much smaller gap. ONE kernel shape, ONE capture: a hypothesis, not a finding, and it is recorded because it runs against the work already done. (e) NO BYTES ARE MEASURED. Every figure is a byte MODEL times an element count derived from launch geometry. The governed-run evidence contract requires measured device-memory traffic reported separately from estimated traffic; there is still none for this trace. (f) THE OVERLAP CHECK IS STILL UNRUN. Named in cycle 10 not_closed (f) and still cheap, and it now matters MORE: if these launches overlap each other or other work, "achieved bandwidth per launch" does not mean what both cycles have assumed. (g) THE ZERO-STORE BYTE MODEL IS UNVERIFIED IN ONE DIRECTION. A full-tensor zero is modelled as a pure 2-byte store with no read-for-ownership. If the hardware does issue one, zero_ moves more traffic than modelled and the ratio falls -- against the refutation, which is the conservative direction, but it is an assumption and not a measurement. (h) THE FIRST _foreach_zero_ LAUNCH OF EACH STEP IS ANOMALOUS (18.497 us against a ~46 us typical) and was not investigated. It is folded into the aggregate, where it makes zero_ look marginally FASTER and therefore the ratio marginally larger. Under 0.5 percent of the class total, but unexplained. (i) ONE TRACE, ONE GEOMETRY, ONE CHECKPOINT, under profiling instrumentation, with no matched-run variation.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF the MAXIMUM achieved read+write bandwidth over all qualifying kernel shapes in this trace EXCEEDS 700 GB/s. A qualifying shape is one whose element count is derivable from launch geometry alone, whose byte model is derivable from its functor signature alone, and whose total modelled traffic exceeds 144 MB (twice this card 72 MB L2, so the working set cannot be cache-resident). Then the 471.7 GB/s point was shape-specific, this host does stream read+write well above it, and fused AdamW at 386.2 GB/s really is far below the machine demonstrated capability -- the framing of cycles 9 through 11 stands and the denominator hypothesis is refuted. SUPPORTED IF the maximum is AT OR BELOW 700 GB/s: the empirical read+write envelope of this host under this instrumentation sits between roughly 472 and 700, fused AdamW at 386.2 is within 1.82x of the best rate anything in this trace achieves, and the "third of streaming rate" framing overstated the opportunity by using a spec denominator. THE 700 FIGURE IS 1.48x THE SINGLE OBSERVED READ+WRITE POINT (471.7). It is set there so that the observed point could be shape-specific by nearly 50 percent and the hypothesis would still be refuted -- the bar is deliberately generous to the incumbent framing, because the finding that would flatter this seat is the one that says three cycles of its own work rested on the wrong denominator. It is not a hardware limit and not a performance target. INCONCLUSIVE IS THE CORRECT VERDICT, NOT A FALLBACK, IF fewer than THREE distinct qualifying shapes clear the 144 MB traffic floor -- an envelope cannot be called from one or two points -- or if the highest-rate qualifying shape turns out to be a pure store or a pure load rather than read+write, in which case it measures a different thing than the fused-AdamW mix and the comparison repeats cycle 11 confound one class over. DISCLOSED BEFORE THE CRITERION: the single data point already in hand (471.7) sits BELOW the 700 bar, so the sketch points at SUPPORTED. The bar is set at 1.48x that point rather than just above it precisely so the criterion is not clearing a height already reached. Every other qualifying shape in the trace is unmeasured. BOTH CLAUSES OF THAT BRANCH FIRE AT ONCE. Exactly ONE distinct shape of the 25 cleared 144 MB: FillFunctor<float>, 256 MB, 6 samples, 582.241 us median, 461.0 GB/s -- and it is a PURE STORE. The criterion required three, and required the top one to be read+write. THE DEFECT IS THE FLOOR I FROZE, AND IT IS MINE. Cycle 11 supplementary observation, the one this whole hypothesis was built from, is a 96 MB read+write shape at 471.7 GB/s. I then froze the floor at 144 MB. The criterion therefore excluded by construction the exact measurement the hypothesis rests on, and I did not notice because I set the floor from a principle (twice the 72 MB L2) instead of from the trace. Counting shapes above the candidate floor is one command and I had the trace in hand for the whole of cycle 11. That is the discriminator-in-hand failure pointed at a criterion instead of at a treatment. LOWERING THE FLOOR NOW IS NOT LICENSED. Moving a frozen threshold after the count comes back inconvenient is choosing the bar to fit the answer -- the defect cycle 9 recorded about denominators and cycle 11 refused about controls. The floor moves in cycle 13 criterion, BEFORE anything runs, with the residency caveat priced rather than dodged. WHAT THE TRACE DID SHOW, REPORTED AND NOT RULING. Exactly two shapes clear the 72 MB L2 at all: the 96 MB read+write at 471.7 GB/s and the 256 MB pure store at 461.0 GB/s. They agree to 1.02x. Every cache-resident shape below the L2 runs 1.8x to 3.7x faster -- the top row reports 1739.9 GB/s, which is 1.73x the published 1008 GB/s DRAM figure and is therefore an L2 rate, not a memory rate. So the floor was the right instrument and only its number was wrong. Both non-resident points land near 465 GB/s, about 46 percent of the published figure, against fused AdamW at 386.2. If that holds, the optimizer block is 1.22x off a demonstrated rate rather than 2.6x off a spec sheet. TWO points, one of them a pure store, is not an envelope, and the criterion said three.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS CYCLE DOES NOT CLOSE, named while the reasoning is fresh. (a) THE DENOMINATOR QUESTION IS ENTIRELY OPEN AND IS NOW THE CAMPAIGN HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM. It decides whether the optimizer block is a 2.6x opportunity or a 1.22x one, and therefore whether ANY optimizer treatment belongs on the critical path. Nothing here answers it; the cycle spent itself discovering that its own admission floor left one point standing. (b) THE 144 MB FLOOR IS REFUTED AS A POPULATION SELECTOR FOR THIS TRACE, WHICH IS A REAL FINDING EVEN THOUGH IT IS ABOUT MY CRITERION RATHER THAN THE HOST. This trace has one shape above 144 MB and two above 72 MB. Any successor selecting on absolute traffic must state its count before freezing. A threshold over a population is checked against that population SIZE first. (c) THE 471.7 FIGURE IS NOT CLEAN AND THIS ROW SAYS SO. 96 MB against a 72 MB L2 is only 1.33x, so a meaningful fraction of that working set can be resident and the figure is an UPPER BOUND on the DRAM rate, inflated by an unmeasured amount. Cycle 11 published it without this caveat; it is added here. (d) THE PURE-STORE CONFOUND IS BACK, ONE CLASS OVER. The only shape clearing the frozen floor is a fill. Comparing a store peak against the fused-AdamW read+write mix is exactly what cycle 11 refused to do, which is why the criterion excluded it in advance. A successor needs a read+write shape above the L2, and this trace contains exactly one. (e) THE TRACE MAY NOT CONTAIN THE ANSWER AT ALL. 2,802 launches were refused for carrying mangled back-references, and some of those are large bf16 multiplies. A demangler-backed admission rule would admit them and could add qualifying shapes -- that is a cheap, named, unrun extension, and it is the most likely route to a third point without inventing a workload. (f) NO BYTES ARE MEASURED, STILL. Every figure is a byte MODEL times a geometry-derived element count, and modelled traffic is an upper bound because a write to an already-read line can be satisfied in L2. The governed-run evidence contract requires measured device-memory traffic reported separately from estimated traffic; there is none for this trace. (g) OCCUPANCY (candidate 3) IS UNTOUCHED BY THIS CYCLE and survives from cycle 11 exactly as it entered -- neither confirmed nor refuted, now by three consecutive cycles. (h) THE OVERLAP CHECK IS STILL UNRUN, named in cycle 10 not_closed (f) and cycle 11 (f). Every per-launch bandwidth figure in this receipt assumes the launch had the memory system to itself. (i) ONE TRACE, ONE GEOMETRY, ONE CHECKPOINT, under profiling instrumentation, with no matched-run variation, and the profiled wall time is dominated by the profiler.

Methods, evidence and limitations
Criterion, frozen before the run
REFUTED IF more than 5 PERCENT of the summed device duration of the measured classes (fused AdamW FusedAdamMathFunctor, _foreach_zero_ ZeroFunctor, and the vectorized_elementwise family) inside the six measured-step windows is covered by the wall-time interval of ANY OTHER device event in the trace -- another kernel or a memcpy, on any stream. Then per-launch bandwidth attribution is materially unsafe, every achieved-rate figure in cycles 9 through 12 is understated by an unbounded amount, and those receipts need an overlap-corrected restatement rather than an extension. THE 5 PERCENT FIGURE IS CHOSEN AGAINST THIS SEAT INTEREST, DELIBERATELY LOW. A shared fraction of 5 percent could still distort an individual launch rate; the bar is set where it is because the campaign has been quoting these rates to three significant figures and comparing them at ratios as tight as 1.02x. A method that survives a 5 percent bar is one those comparisons can bear. It is not a hardware limit and not a target. INCONCLUSIVE IS THE CORRECT VERDICT, NOT A FALLBACK, IF the trace kernel events lack the fields needed to establish concurrency -- specifically if ts or dur is absent or non-numeric on any measured-class event -- or if the six windows contain fewer than two distinct device streams among ALL events, because then non-overlap is a property of how the capture serialised the device rather than evidence about how the work runs. Either way the computation did not discriminate and the ruling says so instead of taking the comfortable branch. DISCLOSED BEFORE THE CRITERION: the sketch points at REFUTED. A prior audit on a different trace of this campaign found 1,426 different-stream overlapping kernel pairs, so overlap is known to occur on this host. Refuting this hypothesis would mean four of my own cycles carry a quantified error, which is the outcome that costs this seat the most and is the reason the bar is set low rather than generously. state\issue1945-receipts\launch-overlap-audit-20260914.md
Result in full
INCONCLUSIVE against the frozen criterion, on the branch the criterion itself names as correct rather than a fallback: "INCONCLUSIVE IS THE CORRECT VERDICT, NOT A FALLBACK, IF the trace kernel events lack the fields needed to establish concurrency ... or if the six windows contain fewer than two distinct device streams among ALL events, because then non-overlap is a property of how the capture serialised the device rather than evidence about how the work runs." THE MEASUREMENT CAME BACK COMPLETELY CLEAN AND IT STILL DOES NOT RULE. Zero shared microseconds. Not one microsecond of one launch of 14,830 measured-class launches (210,338.3 us of device time) overlapped any other device event anywhere in the capture. Zero launches with any recorded overlap. IT DOES NOT RULE BECAUSE EVERY DEVICE EVENT IN THIS TRACE SITS ON ONE STREAM -- stream 7, all 47,446 of them. CUDA serialises work issued to one stream, so zero overlap is what the execution model guarantees, not what the measurement discovered. Calling this SUPPORTED would assert that concurrent execution does not stretch these durations, when nothing in this capture was concurrent and the question was therefore never put. THE SKETCH POINTED AT REFUTED AND WAS WRONG FOR AN INSTRUCTIVE REASON. The disclosure named 1,426 different-stream overlapping kernel pairs found earlier in this campaign. Those were on a DIFFERENT trace, which means the workload can run multi-stream and this capture does not -- so the evidence that motivated the hypothesis is precisely the evidence this trace cannot supply. THE INSTRUMENT REACHES BOTH OTHER BRANCHES, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS VERDICT FALSIFIABLE. On two-stream fixtures identical but for the overlap width, the same code returns REFUTED at 16.6667 percent and SUPPORTED at 1.6667 percent across the 5 percent bar. A third fixture puts that same 16.6667 percent overlap on ONE stream and the code rules INCONCLUSIVE -- proving the branch fires on the stream count and not on the number, which is the whole point of the clause.
Limitations, and what this does not close
WHAT THIS CYCLE ESTABLISHES AND WHAT IT LEAVES OPEN, named while the reasoning is fresh. (a) ESTABLISHED, AS A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A VERDICT: per-launch bandwidth attribution is safe for every figure computed from THIS capture, because nothing in it was concurrent. That retires the not_closed line carried by cycle 10 (f), cycle 11 (f) and cycle 12 (h) FOR THIS TRACE SPECIFICALLY. It says nothing about a trace from a multi-stream configuration. (b) ESTABLISHED, AND IT IS THE MORE VALUABLE HALF: the device work in this step is STRICTLY SERIAL. 47,446 device events, one stream, zero concurrency, across the whole capture. That is the premise of factor-table row 3 -- serialization of bandwidth-bound work behind compute-bound work, measured 37.6 ms, excess ~32 ms, 19 percent of the 4x -- which until now has been an inference from a step decomposition rather than a direct structural observation. It is a statement about what the execution currently DOES; how much of that serial time a second stream could collect is a mechanism question and nothing here measures it. (c) THE HYPOTHESIS ITSELF IS UNRULED AND NEEDS A DIFFERENT TRACE. Whether concurrent execution stretches these durations can only be answered on a capture that has concurrency in it. The campaign has one -- the trace where 1,426 different-stream overlapping pairs were found -- and running this same instrument against it is the cheapest named route, with no new instrument and no card. (d) RECORDED OVERLAP IS NOT BUS CONTENTION. Two concurrent kernels may contend for memory bandwidth to very different degrees. Interval overlap is evidence of concurrent residency and nothing finer, so even a REFUTED on another trace would bound the risk rather than quantify the distortion. (e) ABSENCE OF RECORDED OVERLAP DOES NOT PROVE ISOLATION. Host-side activity, other processes and display work are invisible to this trace. The zero is a zero about what the profiler recorded on this device context. (f) THE ENVELOPE EXTENSION CYCLE 12 OWED IS STILL OWED and is now better motivated, since its per-launch method is validated for this capture: lower the traffic floor from a COUNT over the trace instead of the 2x-L2 principle, and add demangler-backed admission for the 2,802 launches refused for mangled back-references.
